```
     1→# Architecture
     2→
     3→CipherBox implements layered, zero-knowledge encryption where **all user data is encrypted client-side before leaving the device**. The server and storage layer never see plaintext — not file contents, not file names, not folder names, not timestamps, not plaintext file sizes (encrypted blob sizes are visible for storage accounting).
     4→
     5→## System Overview
     6→
     7→```text
     8→User Device (Web/Desktop)
     9→        ↓ Auth (4 methods)
    10→CipherBox Backend (JWT)
    11→        ↓
    12→Web3Auth Network (Key Derivation)
    13→        ↓ ECDSA Private Key (RAM only!)
    14→User Device ← Vault Data ← PostgreSQL
    15→        ↓ Encrypted Keys
    16→IPFS (Kubo) ← Encrypted Files
    17→        ↑
    18→TEE (Phala/Nitro) ← IPNS Republish (every 6h)
    19→```
    20→
    21→**Key Properties:**
    22→
    23→- Same user identity → same keypair → same vault (auth methods share a vault when they resolve to the same CipherBox `userId`; see [Authentication Architecture](AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md))
    24→- TEE republishes IPNS records even when all devices are offline
    25→
    26→## Key Derivation
    27→
    28→Two paths produce the same `VaultKey` (a secp256k1 keypair):
    29→
    30→```text
    31→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    32→│                       KEY DERIVATION                                │
    33→├─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┤
    34→│  Web3Auth (Social Login)│  Web3Auth (External Wallet via SIWE)     │
    35→│                         │                                           │
    36→│  Google/Email/etc.      │  1. Wallet signs SIWE (EIP-4361) message │
    37→│        ↓                │  2. Backend verifies SIWE, issues JWT    │
    38→│  Web3Auth Network       │  3. Web3Auth Core Kit validates JWT      │
    39→│        ↓                │  4. Web3Auth Network                     │
    40→│  secp256k1 keypair      │        ↓                                  │
    41→│  (deterministic)        │  secp256k1 keypair (deterministic)       │
    42→│                         │                                           │
    43→│                         │                                           │
    44→├─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┤
    45→│                              ↓                                      │
    46→│                   VaultKey (secp256k1)                               │
    47→│          ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐                    │
    48→│          │ Private Key: 32 bytes (RAM only!)   │                    │
    49→│          │ Public Key:  65 bytes (uncompressed) │                   │
    50→│          └─────────────────────────────────────┘                    │
    51→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    52→```
    53→
    54→## Encryption Hierarchy
    55→
    56→Every key below the VaultKey is **randomly generated** (not derived) and **ECIES-wrapped** with the user's public key. Compromising one file key reveals nothing about other file keys.
    57→
    58→```text
    59→    VaultKey (secp256k1 keypair)
    60→    │
    61→    │  ECIES-unwrap
    62→    ├──────────────────────────────────────────┐
    63→    │                                          │
    64→    ▼                                          ▼
    65→ rootFolderKey (random 32B)          rootIpnsPrivateKey (Ed25519)
    66→    │                                          │
    67→    │  AES-256-GCM decrypt                     │  Signs IPNS records
    68→    ▼                                          ▼
    69→ Root Folder Metadata (encrypted JSON)     IPNS publish/resolve
    70→    │
    71→    │  Contains per-child entries:
    72→    │
    73→    ├── File Entries ──────────────────────────────────────────┐
    74→    │     name (encrypted)        ◄── only visible after       │
    75→    │     size (encrypted)            decrypting metadata       │
    76→    │     timestamps (encrypted)                                │
    77→    │     fileKeyEncrypted ──── ECIES-unwrap ──► fileKey (32B) │
    78→    │     fileIv (12B)                              │           │
    79→    │     cid (IPFS ref)            AES-256-GCM decrypt        │
    80→    │                                               ▼          │
    81→    │                                        File Contents     │
    82→    │                                                          │
    83→    ├── Subfolder Entries ─────────────────────────────────────┤
    84→    │     name (encrypted)        ◄── only visible after       │
    85→    │     timestamps (encrypted)      decrypting metadata      │
    86→    │     folderKeyEncrypted ── ECIES-unwrap ──► folderKey     │
    87→    │     ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted ─ ECIES-unwrap ► ipnsKey     │
    88→    │     ipnsName (k51...)                                    │
    89→    │          │                                               │
    90→    │          ▼                                               │
    91→    │     Subfolder Metadata (same structure, recursive)       │
    92→    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    93→```
    94→
    95→## Visibility Model
    96→
    97→```text
    98→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    99→│                    FULLY ENCRYPTED                               │
   100→│   (requires user's private key + folder key to access)          │
   101→│                                                                  │
   102→│   ✓ File contents              ✓ File names                     │
   103→│   ✓ Folder names               ✓ Folder structure / child list  │
   104→│   ✓ File sizes                 ✓ Creation timestamps            │
   105→│   ✓ Modification timestamps    ✓ All encryption keys            │
   106→│   ✓ IPNS private keys          ✓ File-to-folder relationships   │
   107→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
   108→│                    VISIBLE (Plaintext)                           │
   109→│   (required for IPFS/IPNS protocol operation)                   │
   110→│                                                                  │
   111→│   • IPFS CIDs (content-addressed hashes, no semantic meaning)  │
   112→│   • IPNS names (k51... public identifiers for folders)         │
   113→│   • Encrypted blob sizes (approximate original sizes)          │
   114→│   • Encryption IVs (required for decryption, not secret)       │
   115→│   • User's secp256k1 public key                                │
   116→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
   117→│                    NEVER STORED (RAM Only)                       │
   118→│                                                                  │
   119→│   • User's private key          • Decrypted file names          │
   120→│   • Decrypted folder metadata   • Decrypted file contents       │
   121→│   • Plaintext file/folder keys  • Wallet signatures             │
   122→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   123→```
   124→
   125→## Cryptographic Primitives
   126→
   127→| Purpose                             | Algorithm                  | Parameters                               |
   128→| :---------------------------------- | :------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- |
   129→| File & metadata encryption          | AES-256-GCM                | 256-bit key, 96-bit IV, 128-bit auth tag |
   130→| Key wrapping                        | ECIES (secp256k1)          | Ephemeral keypair + AES-GCM              |
   131→| Deterministic key derivation (IPNS) | HKDF-SHA256                | 32-byte output, context-specific salt    |
   132→| IPNS record signing                 | Ed25519                    | 32-byte seed, 64-byte signatures         |
   133→| Random generation                   | `crypto.getRandomValues()` | CSPRNG (Web Crypto API)                  |
   134→
   135→## Data Flows
   136→
   137→### File Upload
   138→
   139→```text
   140→  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   141→  │                    FILE UPLOAD FLOW                           │
   142→  │                                                              │
   143→  │  1. User selects "document.pdf"                              │
   144→  │         │                                                    │
   145→  │         ▼                                                    │
   146→  │  2. Generate random fileKey (32 bytes)                       │
   147→  │     Generate random IV (12 bytes)                            │
   148→  │         │                                                    │
   149→  │         ▼                                                    │
   150→  │  3. AES-256-GCM encrypt(plaintext, fileKey, IV)              │
   151→  │     → ciphertext ‖ auth_tag (16 bytes)                       │
   152→  │         │                                                    │
   153→  │         ▼                                                    │
   154→  │  4. ECIES wrap(fileKey, userPublicKey)                        │
   155→  │     → ephemeral_pubkey ‖ wrapped_key ‖ tag                   │
   156→  │         │                                                    │
   157→  │         ▼                                                    │
   158→  │  5. Clear plaintext fileKey from memory                      │
   159→  │         │                                                    │
   160→  │         ▼                                                    │
   161→  │  6. Upload encrypted blob → IPFS (Kubo) → returns CID     │
   162→  │         │                                                    │
   163→  │         ▼                                                    │
   164→  │  7. Add to folder metadata:                                  │
   165→  │       { name, cid, fileKeyEncrypted, fileIv, size, ... }     │
   166→  │         │                                                    │
   167→  │         ▼                                                    │
   168→  │  8. Re-encrypt folder metadata with folderKey (AES-256-GCM)  │
   169→  │         │                                                    │
   170→  │         ▼                                                    │
   171→  │  9. Upload encrypted metadata → IPFS → new CID              │
   172→  │         │                                                    │
   173→  │         ▼                                                    │
   174→  │ 10. Publish IPNS record: /ipns/k51... → /ipfs/<new CID>     │
   175→  │     (signed with folder's Ed25519 private key)               │
   176→  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   177→```
   178→
   179→## Defense in Depth
   180→
   181→Each file key is protected by multiple nested layers. An attacker must break through all layers to access any file content:
   182→
   183→```text
   184→   File Content
   185→     └─ encrypted with ──► fileKey (random, unique per file)
   186→         └─ ECIES-wrapped with ──► User's Public Key
   187→             └─ stored inside ──► Folder Metadata
   188→                 └─ encrypted with ──► folderKey (random, unique per folder)
   189→                     └─ ECIES-wrapped with ──► User's Public Key
   190→                         └─ stored on ──► Server (zero-knowledge)
   191→```
   192→
   193→## Threat Model
   194→
   195→With full access to IPFS and the CipherBox server but without the user's private key:
   196→
   197→```text
   198→  IPFS (public network):
   199→
   200→    /ipfs/bafybei3a7x...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)
   201→    /ipfs/bafybei9f2k...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)
   202→    /ipfs/bafybeiqw8m...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)
   203→
   204→  Without the user's private key:
   205→    ✗ Cannot read file contents
   206→    ✗ Cannot read file or folder names
   207→    ✗ Cannot determine folder structure
   208→    ✗ Cannot read timestamps or file sizes
   209→    ✗ Cannot determine which blobs are files vs. folders
   210→    ✓ Can see encrypted blob sizes (approximates original size)
   211→    ✓ Can see IPNS update frequency (usage pattern)
   212→```
   213→
   214→## Key Design Decisions
   215→
   216→### 1. Web3Auth for Key Derivation
   217→
   218→```text
   219→Any auth method → CipherBox backend resolves userId → Web3Auth → Same ECDSA keypair
   220→```
   221→
   222→### 2. Layered Encryption
   223→
   224→```text
   225→File (AES-256-GCM) → Metadata (AES-256-GCM) → Keys (ECIES)
   226→```
   227→
   228→### 3. Per-Folder IPNS
   229→
   230→```text
   231→Root IPNS → Folder1 IPNS → Folder2 IPNS (modular sharing-ready)
   232→```
   233→
   234→### 4. IPNS Polling Sync
   235→
   236→```text
   237→30s polling, no push infrastructure (MVP simple)
   238→```
   239→
   240→### 5. Zero-Knowledge Keys
   241→
   242→```text
   243→Server holds: Encrypted root key only
   244→Client holds: Private key (RAM only)
   245→```
   246→
   247→### 6. TEE-Based IPNS Republishing
   248→
   249→```text
   250→IPNS records expire after ~24h → backend scheduler triggers republish every 6h
   251→Client encrypts ipnsPrivateKey with TEE public key (ECIES)
   252→TEE decrypts in hardware, signs, zeroes key immediately
   253→Providers: Phala Cloud (primary) / AWS Nitro (fallback)
   254→```
   255→
   256→## User Journey Example
   257→
   258→```text
   259→1. Signup (Google) → Web3Auth derives KeyA
   260→2. Upload file → Encrypt → IPFS CID → IPNS publish
   261→3. Phone login (Email) → Web3Auth derives KeyA (same!)
   262→4. Phone polls IPNS → Sees file → Downloads & decrypts
   263→5. Export vault → JSON with CIDs + encrypted root key
   264→6. CipherBox gone? → Use export + private key → Full recovery
   265→```
   266→
   267→## Further Reading
   268→
   269→- [Frozen Specifications](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/) — PRD, Technical Architecture, API Specification, Data Flows, Client Specification
   270→- [Authentication Architecture](AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md) — detailed auth flow documentation
   271→- [Metadata Schemas](METADATA_SCHEMAS.md) — all metadata object schemas with field tables
   272→- [Vault Export Format](VAULT_EXPORT_FORMAT.md) — export/recovery data format
   273→
```

docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md:177:**Key distinction from v1 FileEntry:** FilePointer does not contain `cid`, `fileKeyEncrypted`, `fileIv`, `encryptionMode`, or `size`. All file crypto material is in the per-file `FileMetadata` record, enabling file content updates without touching folder metadata.
docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md:209:**Storage:** IPFS (as JSON envelope `{iv, data}`), addressed via the file's own IPNS name. For new files, the IPNS keypair is randomly generated and stored in the parent's `FilePointer.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted`. For legacy files, it is derived via HKDF from `privateKey + fileId` (see [Section 14](#14-ipns-key-derivation-summary)).
docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md:387:| FilePointer        | `file/types.ts`     | `crypto/folder.rs`                     | Both platforms  |
docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md:413:**Legacy: Per-file IPNS (HKDF fallback):** Files created before v0.14.0 use `cipherbox-file-ipns-v1:{fileId}` as the HKDF info string. This derivation path is retained for backward compatibility and used when `FilePointer.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted` is absent.
docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md:431:| Per-file IPNS | `FilePointer.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted` | `packages/crypto/src/file/derive-ipns.ts` |
docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md:433:**Migration:** Legacy files without `ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted` fall back to HKDF derivation. Lazy migration writes the encrypted key when folder metadata is next published. Recovery traverses the folder tree via `fileMetaIpnsName` and never derives IPNS names independently, so the random-key approach is safe.

Publish IPNS record: /ipns/k51... → /ipfs/<new CID>     │"
}
```

Wallet signs SIWE (EIP-4361) message │\n+│        ↓                │  2. Backend verifies SIWE, issues JWT    │\n+│  Web3Auth Network       │  3. Web3Auth Core Kit validates JWT      │\n+│        ↓                │  4. Web3Auth Network                     │\n+│  secp256k1 keypair      │        ↓                                  │\n+│  (deterministic)        │  secp256k1 keypair (deterministic)       │\n+│                         │                                           │\n+│                         │                                           │\n+├─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┤\n+│                              ↓                                      │\n+│                   VaultKey (secp256k1)                               │\n+│          ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐                    │\n+│          │ Private Key: 32 bytes (RAM only!)   │                    │\n+│          │ Public Key:  65 bytes (uncompressed) │                   │\n+│          └─────────────────────────────────────┘                    │\n+└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n+```\n+\n+## Encryption Hierarchy\n+\n+Every key below the VaultKey is **randomly generated** (not derived) and **ECIES-wrapped** with the user's public key.             │\n+    │  AES-256-GCM decrypt                     │  Signs IPNS records\n+    ▼                                          ▼\n+ Root Folder Metadata (encrypted JSON)     IPNS      │                                               │\n+    │          ▼                                               │\n+    │     Subfolder Metadata (same structure, recursive)       │\n+           │\n+│                                                                  │\n+│   • IPFS CIDs (content-addressed hashes, no semantic meaning)  │\n+│   • IPNS names (k51... public identifiers    | Algorithm                  | Parameters                               |\n+| :---------------------------------- | :------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- |\n+| File      | 32-byte seed, 64-byte signatures         |\n+| Random generation                   | `crypto.getRandomValues()` | CSPRNG (Web Crypto API)                  |\n+\n+## Data Flows\n+\n+### File                          │\n+  │  1. Re-encrypt folder metadata with folderKey (AES-256-GCM)  │\n+  │         │                                                    │\n+  │         ▼                                                    │\n+  │  9. Upload encrypted metadata → IPFS → new CID              │\n+  │         │                                                    │\n+  │         ▼                                                    │\n+  │ 10. The server and storage layer never see plaintext — not file contents, not file names, not folder names, not timestamps, not plaintext file sizes (encrypted blob sizes are visible for storage accounting).\n+\n+## System Overview\n+\n+```text\n+User Device (Web/Desktop)\n+        ↓ Auth (4 methods)\n+CipherBox Backend (JWT)\n+        ↓\n+Web3Auth Network (Key Derivation)\n+        ↓ ECDSA Private Key (RAM only!)\n+User Device ← Vault Data ← PostgreSQL\n+        ↓ Encrypted Keys\n+IPFS (Kubo) ← Encrypted Files\n+        ↑\n+TEE (Phala/Nitro) ← IPNS Republish (every 6h)\n+```\n+\n+**Key Properties:**\n+\n+- Same user identity → same keypair → same vault (auth methods share a vault when they resolve to the same CipherBox `userId`; see [Authentication Architecture](AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md))\n+- TEE republishes IPNS records even when all devices are offline\n+\n+## Key Derivation\n+\n+Two paths produce the same `VaultKey` (a secp256k1 keypair):\n+\n+```text\n+┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n+│                       KEY DERIVATION                                │\n+├─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┤\n+│  Web3Auth (Social Login)│  Web3Auth (External Wallet via SIWE)     │\n+│                         │                                           │\n+│  Google/Email/etc.      │  1. Wallet signs SIWE (EIP-4361) message │\n+│        ↓                │  2. Backend verifies SIWE, issues JWT    │\n+│  Web3Auth Network       │  3. Web3Auth Core Kit validates JWT      │\n+│        ↓                │  4. Web3Auth Network                     │\n+│  secp256k1 keypair      │        ↓                                  │\n+│  (deterministic)        │  secp256k1 keypair (deterministic)       │\n+│                         │                                           │\n+│                         │                                           │\n+├─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┤\n+│                              ↓                                      │\n+│                   VaultKey (secp256k1)                               │\n+│          ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐                    │\n+│          │ Private Key: 32 bytes (RAM only!)   │                    │\n+│          │ Public Key:  65 bytes (uncompressed) │                   │\n+│          └─────────────────────────────────────┘                    │\n+└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n+```\n+\n+## Encryption Hierarchy\n+\n+Every key below the VaultKey is **randomly generated** (not derived) and **ECIES-wrapped** with the user's public key. Compromising one file key reveals nothing about other file keys.\n+\n+```text\n+    VaultKey (secp256k1 keypair)\n+    │\n+    │  ECIES-unwrap\n+    ├──────────────────────────────────────────┐\n+    │                                          │\n+    ▼                                          ▼\n+ rootFolderKey (random 32B)          rootIpnsPrivateKey (Ed25519)\n+    │                                          │\n+    │  AES-256-GCM decrypt                     │  Signs IPNS records\n+    ▼                                          ▼\n+ Root Folder Metadata (encrypted JSON)     IPNS publish/resolve\n+    │\n+    │  Contains per-child entries:\n+    │\n+    ├── File Entries ──────────────────────────────────────────┐\n+    │     name (encrypted)        ◄── only visible after       │\n+    │     size (encrypted)            decrypting metadata       │\n+    │     timestamps (encrypted)                                │\n+    │     fileKeyEncrypted ──── ECIES-unwrap ──► fileKey (32B) │\n+    │     fileIv (12B)                              │           │\n+    │     cid (IPFS ref)            AES-256-GCM decrypt        │\n+    │                                               ▼          │\n+    │                                        File Contents     │\n+    │                                                          │\n+    ├── Subfolder Entries ─────────────────────────────────────┤\n+    │     name (encrypted)        ◄── only visible after       │\n+    │     timestamps (encrypted)      decrypting metadata      │\n+    │     folderKeyEncrypted ── ECIES-unwrap ──► folderKey     │\n+    │     ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted ─ ECIES-unwrap ► ipnsKey     │\n+    │     ipnsName (k51...)                                    │\n+    │          │                                               │\n+    │          ▼                                               │\n+    │     Subfolder Metadata (same structure, recursive)       │\n+    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n+```\n+\n+## Visibility Model\n+\n+```text\n+┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n+│                    FULLY ENCRYPTED                               │\n+│   (requires user's private key + folder key to access)          │\n+│                                                                  │\n+│   ✓ File contents              ✓ File names                     │\n+│   ✓ Folder names               ✓ Folder structure / child list  │\n+│   ✓ File sizes                 ✓ Creation timestamps            │\n+│   ✓ Modification timestamps    ✓ All encryption keys            │\n+│   ✓ IPNS private keys          ✓ File-to-folder relationships   │\n+├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n+│                    VISIBLE (Plaintext)                           │\n+│   (required for IPFS/IPNS protocol operation)                   │\n+│                                                                  │\n+│   • IPFS CIDs (content-addressed hashes, no semantic meaning)  │\n+│   • IPNS names (k51... public identifiers for folders)         │\n+│   • Encrypted blob sizes (approximate original sizes)          │\n+│   • Encryption IVs (required for decryption, not secret)       │\n+│   • User's secp256k1 public key                                │\n+├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n+│                    NEVER STORED (RAM Only)                       │\n+│                                                                  │\n+│   • User's private key          • Decrypted file names          │\n+│   • Decrypted folder metadata   • Decrypted file contents       │\n+│   • Plaintext file/folder keys  • Wallet signatures             │\n+└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n+```\n+\n+## Cryptographic Primitives\n+\n+| Purpose                             | Algorithm                  | Parameters                               |\n+| :---------------------------------- | :------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- |\n+| File & metadata encryption          | AES-256-GCM                | 256-bit key, 96-bit IV, 128-bit auth tag |\n+| Key wrapping                        | ECIES (secp256k1)          | Ephemeral keypair + AES-GCM              |\n+| Deterministic key derivation (IPNS) | HKDF-SHA256                | 32-byte output, context-specific salt    |\n+| IPNS record signing                 | Ed25519                    | 32-byte seed, 64-byte signatures         |\n+| Random generation                   | `crypto.getRandomValues()` | CSPRNG (Web Crypto API)                  |\n+\n+## Data Flows\n+\n+### File Upload\n+\n+```text\n+  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n+  │                    FILE UPLOAD FLOW                           │\n+  │                                                              │\n+  │  1. User selects \"document.pdf\"                              │\n+  │         │                                                    │\n+  │         ▼                                                    │\n+  │  2. Generate random fileKey (32 bytes)                       │\n+  │     Generate random IV (12 bytes)                            │\n+  │         │                                                    │\n+  │         ▼                                                    │\n+  │  3. AES-256-GCM encrypt(plaintext, fileKey, IV)              │\n+  │     → ciphertext ‖ auth_tag (16 bytes)                       │\n+  │         │                                                    │\n+  │         ▼                                                    │\n+  │  4. ECIES wrap(fileKey, userPublicKey)                        │\n+  │     → ephemeral_pubkey ‖ wrapped_key ‖ tag                   │\n+  │         │                                                    │\n+  │         ▼                                                    │\n+  │  5. Clear plaintext fileKey from memory                      │\n+  │         │                                                    │\n+  │         ▼                                                    │\n+  │  6. Upload encrypted blob → IPFS (Kubo) → returns CID     │\n+  │         │                                                    │\n+  │         ▼                                                    │\n+  │  7. Add to folder metadata:                                  │\n+  │       { name, cid, fileKeyEncrypted, fileIv, size, ... }     │\n+  │         │                                                    │\n+  │         ▼                                                    │\n+  │  8. Re-encrypt folder metadata with folderKey (AES-256-GCM)  │\n+  │         │                                                    │\n+  │         ▼                                                    │\n+  │  9. Upload encrypted metadata → IPFS → new CID              │\n+  │         │                                                    │\n+  │         ▼                                                    │\n+  │ 10. Publish IPNS record: /ipns/k51... → /ipfs/<new CID>     │\n+  │     (signed with folder's Ed25519 private key)               │\n+  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n+```\n+\n+## Defense in Depth\n+\n+Each file key is protected by multiple nested layers. An attacker must break through all layers to access any file content:\n+\n+```text\n+   File Content\n+     └─ encrypted with ──► fileKey (random, unique per file)\n+         └─ ECIES-wrapped with ──► User's Public Key\n+             └─ stored inside ──► Folder Metadata\n+                 └─ encrypted with ──► folderKey (random, unique per folder)\n+                     └─ ECIES-wrapped with ──► User's Public Key\n+                         └─ stored on ──► Server (zero-knowledge)\n+```\n+\n+## Threat Model\n+\n+With full access to IPFS and the CipherBox server but without the user's private key:\n+\n+```text\n+  IPFS (public network):\n+\n+    /ipfs/bafybei3a7x...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)\n+    /ipfs/bafybei9f2k...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)\n+    /ipfs/bafybeiqw8m...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)\n+\n+  Without the user's private key:\n+    ✗ Cannot read file contents\n+    ✗ Cannot read file or folder names\n+    ✗ Cannot determine folder structure\n+    ✗ Cannot read timestamps or file sizes\n+    ✗ Cannot determine which blobs are files vs. folders\n+    ✓ Can see encrypted blob sizes (approximates original size)\n+    ✓ Can see IPNS update frequency (usage pattern)\n+```\n+\n+## Key Design Decisions\n+\n+### 1. Web3Auth for Key Derivation\n+\n+```text\n+Any auth method → CipherBox backend resolves userId → Web3Auth → Same ECDSA keypair\n+```\n+\n+### 2. Layered Encryption\n+\n+```text\n+File (AES-256-GCM) → Metadata (AES-256-GCM) → Keys (ECIES)\n+```\n+\n+### 3. Per-Folder IPNS\n+\n+```text\n+Root IPNS → Folder1 IPNS → Folder2 IPNS (modular sharing-ready)\n+```\n+\n+### 4. IPNS Polling Sync\n+\n+```text\n+30s polling, no push infrastructure (MVP simple)\n+```\n+\n+### 5. Zero-Knowledge Keys\n+\n+```text\n+Server holds: Encrypted root key only\n+Client holds: Private key (RAM only)\n+```\n+\n+### 6. TEE-Based IPNS Republishing\n+\n+```text\n+IPNS records expire after ~24h → backend scheduler triggers republish every 6h\n+Client encrypts ipnsPrivateKey with TEE public key (ECIES)\n+TEE decrypts in hardware, signs, zeroes key immediately\n+Providers: Phala Cloud (primary) / AWS Nitro (fallback)","path":"docs/ARCHITECTURE.md","commit_id":"16f15e4ead5bcbd05a9fbf3e88e5b89204b470fa","original_commit_id":"16f15e4ead5bcbd05a9fbf3e88e5b89204b470fa","user":{"login":"FSM1","id":12774278,"node_id":"MDQ6VXNlcjEyNzc0Mjc4","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/12774278?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1","html_url":"https://github.com/FSM1","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/received_events","type":"User","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false},"body":"Fixed in 692750a. {"url":"https://api.github.com/repos/FSM1/cipher-box/pulls/comments/2893085453","pull_request_review_id":3900464529,"id":2893085453,"node_id":"PRRC_kwDOQ6DXvM6scPsN","diff_hunk":"@@ -2,399 +2,109 @@\n   <img src=\"./cipherbox logo.png\" alt=\"CipherBox Logo\" width=\"450\"/>\n </p>\n \n-# CipherBox - README.md\n-\n-## Privacy-first cloud storage with decentralized persistence\n+<h3 align=\"center\">Privacy-first encrypted cloud storage with decentralized persistence</h3>\n \n ---\n \n-## 📄 Overview\n-\n-**CipherBox is a technology demonstrator** showcasing privacy-first cloud storage with decentralized persistence. It is **not intended as a commercial product** but as a proof-of-concept for:\n-\n-- **Zero-knowledge client-side encryption**\n-- **Decentralized storage via IPFS/IPNS**\n-- **Deterministic key derivation via Web3Auth**\n-- **Cross-device sync without server-side key access**\n-\n-**CipherBox** demonstrates:\n+## What is CipherBox\n \n-- **IPFS/IPNS** for decentralized, redundant storage\n-- **Web3Auth** for deterministic key derivation across multiple auth methods\n-- **AES-256-GCM + ECIES secp256k1** for layered E2E encryption\n-- **React web UI** + **FUSE desktop mount** (macOS v1)\n-- **Automatic multi-device sync** via IPNS polling\n-- **TEE-based IPNS republishing** via Phala Cloud / AWS Nitro (zero-knowledge)\n-\n----\n+CipherBox is a **technology demonstrator** for privacy-first cloud storage. All encryption happens client-side — the server never sees plaintext data, file names, or encryption keys. Files are stored on IPFS for decentralized persistence, keys are derived deterministically via Web3Auth so the same user always gets the same vault regardless of login method, and data can be exported for independent recovery without CipherBox.\n \n ## Acknowledgements\n \n This project is inspired by discussions and planning while working on [ChainSafe Files](https://github.com/chainsafe/ui-monorepo). A massive shout-out to all the colleagues I got to work with on the original ChainSafe Files project, who unknowingly contributed to this phoenix rising out of the ashes.\n \n----\n-\n-## 🎯 Vision\n-\n-**Replace Google Drive/Dropbox with:**\n-\n-```text\n-✓ Client-side encryption (server never sees plaintext)\n-✓ User-held keys (zero-knowledge guarantee)\n-✓ Decentralized storage (no vendor lock-in)\n-✓ Data portability (export vault, recover independently)\n-✓ Multi-device sync (automatic via IPFS)\n-✓ Transparent UX (hide IPFS complexity)\n-```\n-\n----\n-\n-## 📦 MVP Scope (v1.0)\n-\n-### ✅ **Included**\n-\n-```text\n-Auth: Email/Password, OAuth, Magic Link, External Wallet → Web3Auth key derivation\n-Storage: IPFS via Pinata (v1), per-folder IPNS entries\n-Encryption: AES-256-GCM files + ECIES key wrapping\n-Web UI: React file browser, drag-drop, folder ops\n-Desktop: macOS FUSE mount + background sync\n-Sync: IPNS polling (~30s eventual consistency)\n-TEE Republishing: Phala Cloud (primary) / AWS Nitro (fallback), every 3h\n-Portability: Vault export + independent recovery\n-```\n-\n-### ⏱️ **Deferred**\n-\n-```text\n-v1.1: Billing, Linux/Windows desktop, mobile apps\n-v2: File versioning, folder sharing, search\n-```\n-\n----\n-\n-## 🏗️ Technology Stack\n-\n-| Component          | Technology                    | Why                               |\n-| :----------------- | :---------------------------- | :-------------------------------- |\n-| **Frontend**       | React 18 + TypeScript         | Modern crypto UI                  |\n-| **Web Crypto**     | Web Crypto API                | Native browser encryption         |\n-| **Backend**        | Node.js + NestJS + TypeScript | Type-safe APIs                    |\n-| **Database**       | PostgreSQL                    | ACID audit trail                  |\n-| **Key Derivation** | Web3Auth Network              | Deterministic across auth methods |\n-| **Storage**        | IPFS via Pinata               | Redundant, decentralized          |\n-| **Desktop**        | Tauri/Electron + FUSE         | Transparent file access           |\n-| **TEE**            | Phala Cloud / AWS Nitro AES-256-GCM encrypt(plaintext, fileKey, IV)              │\n-  │     → ciphertext ‖ auth_tag (16 bytes)                       │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  4. ECIES wrap(fileKey, userPublicKey)                        │\n-  │     → ephemeral_pubkey ‖ wrapped_key ‖ tag                   │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  5. Clear plaintext fileKey from memory                      │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  6. Upload encrypted blob → Pinata → IPFS → returns CID     │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  7. Add to folder metadata:                                  │\n-  │       { name, cid, fileKeyEncrypted, fileIv, size, ... }     │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  8. Re-encrypt folder metadata with folderKey (AES-256-GCM)  │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  9. Upload encrypted metadata → IPFS → new CID              │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │ 10. Publish IPNS record: /ipns/k51... → /ipfs/<new CID>     │\n-  │     (signed with folder's Ed25519 private key)               │\n-  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n-```\n-\n-### Defense in Depth\n-\n-Each file key is protected by multiple nested layers. An attacker must break through all layers to access any file content:\n-\n-```text\n-   File Content\n-     └─ encrypted with ──► fileKey (random, unique per file)\n-         └─ ECIES-wrapped with ──► User's Public Key\n-             └─ stored inside ──► Folder Metadata\n-                 └─ encrypted with ──► folderKey (random, unique per folder)\n-                     └─ ECIES-wrapped with ──► User's Public Key\n-                         └─ stored on ──► Server (zero-knowledge)\n-```\n-\n-### What an Attacker Sees\n-\n-With full access to IPFS and the CipherBox server but without the user's private key:\n-\n-```text\n-  IPFS (public network):\n-\n-    /ipfs/bafybei3a7x...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)\n-    /ipfs/bafybei9f2k...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)\n-    /ipfs/bafybeiqw8m...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)\n-\n-  Without the user's private key:\n-    ✗ Cannot read file contents\n-    ✗ Cannot read file or folder names\n-    ✗ Cannot determine folder structure\n-    ✗ Cannot read timestamps or file sizes\n-    ✗ Cannot determine which blobs are files vs. folders\n-    ✓ Can see encrypted blob sizes (approximates original size)\n-    ✓ Can see IPNS update frequency (usage pattern)\n-```\n-\n----\n-\n-## 📊 6 Key Decisions\n-\n-### 1. **Web3Auth for Key Derivation**\n-\n-```text\n-Email/Password/OAuth/Magic Link/External Wallet → Web3Auth → Same ECDSA keypair\n-```\n+- **Authentication** — Web3Auth MPC Core Kit: email OTP, Google OAuth, magic link, external wallet. MFA via device factor. Same user identity = same vault.\n+- **Encryption** — Client-side AES-256-GCM for files and metadata. ECIES secp256k1 key wrapping (per-file, per-folder random keys). Streaming AES-256-CTR for large media files.\n+- **File Management** — Upload, download, rename, move, delete. Nested folders up to 20 levels. Drag-and-drop.\n+- **Sync** — Multi-device via IPNS polling (~30s). Conflict detection and resolution.\n+- **Desktop** — macOS via Tauri v2 + FUSE-T virtual filesystem mount. Background sync with system tray.\n+- **TEE Republishing** — Automatic IPNS record refresh every 6 hours via Phala Cloud. Zero-knowledge: keys decrypted only inside hardware enclaves.\n+- **Data Portability** — Full vault export (JSON + encrypted blobs). Standalone recovery with private key — no CipherBox required.\n \n-### 2. **Layered Encryption**\n+### Planned (v1.1+)\n \n-```text\n-File (AES-256-GCM) → Metadata (AES-256-GCM) → Keys (ECIES)\n-```\n+- **Sharing** — User-to-user sharing with ECIES re-wrapping. Link sharing with time-limited access tokens.\n+- **Search** — Client-side encrypted search index across file and folder names.\n+- **Versioning** — File history with point-in-time restore.\n+- **Recycle Bin** — 30-day soft-delete with restore.\n+- **Desktop (Windows & Linux)** — WinFSP / libfuse support.\n \n-### 3. **Per-Folder IPNS**\n+## Architecture Overview\n \n ```text\n-Root IPNS → Folder1 IPNS → Folder2 IPNS (modular sharing-ready)\n+┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────┐     ┌───────────────┐\n+│  Web / Desktop│────▶│  CipherBox   │────▶│  PostgreSQL   │\n+│   (Client)   │     │    API       │     │  (Metadata)   │\n+└──────┬───────┘     └──────┬───────┘     └───────────────┘\n+       │                    │\n+       │ Encrypted          │ Relay\n+       │ blobs              │\n+       ▼                    ▼\n+┌──────────────┐     ┌───────────────┐\n+│    IPFS      │     │  TEE Worker   │\n+│  (Storage)   │     │ (IPNS Refresh)│\n+└──────────────┘     └───────────────┘\n ```\n \n-### 4. **IPNS Polling Sync**\n+The client encrypts everything locally. The API is a zero-knowledge relay — it stores encrypted keys and routes IPFS/IPNS operations but never accesses plaintext. For full cryptographic details, see [docs/ARCHITECTURE.md](docs/ARCHITECTURE.md).\n \n-```text\n-30s polling, no push infrastructure (MVP simple)\n-```\n+## Tech Stack\n \n-### 5. **Zero-Knowledge Keys**\n+| Component          | Technology                                    |\n+| :----------------- | :-------------------------------------------- |\n+| **Frontend**       | React 18 + TypeScript + Tailwind CSS          |\n+| **Backend**        | Node.js + NestJS + TypeScript                 |\n+| **Database**       | PostgreSQL 16                                 |\n+| **Job Queue**      | BullMQ + Redis                                |\n+| **Key Derivation** | Web3Auth MPC Core Kit                         |\n+| **Storage**        | IPFS via Kubo                                 |\n+| **Desktop**        | Tauri v2 + FUSE-T / WinFSP                    |\n+| **TEE**            | Phala Cloud (IPNS republishing)               |\n+| **Crypto**         | Web Crypto API (AES-256-GCM, ECIES secp256k1) |\n \n-```text\n-Server holds: Encrypted root key only\n-Client holds: Private key (RAM only)\n-```\n-\n-### 6. **TEE-Based IPNS Republishing**\n+## Project Structure\n \n ```text\n-IPNS records expire after ~24h → TEE republishes every 3h\n-Client encrypts ipnsPrivateKey with TEE public key (ECIES)\n-TEE decrypts in hardware, signs, zeroes key immediately\n-Providers: Phala Cloud (primary) / AWS Nitro (fallback)\n+cipher-box/\n+├── apps/\n+│   ├── api/              # NestJS backend\n+│   ├── web/              # React frontend\n+│   └── desktop/          # Tauri v2 desktop app\n+├── packages/\n+│   ├── crypto/           # Shared encryption library\n+│   └── api-client/       #      7→CipherBox is a **technology demonstrator** in early development. Currently, only a **Proof of Concept (PoC) console harness** exists in `/00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/`. The full production system (web app, desktop app, backend) is specified in documentation but not yet implemented.
     8→
     9→## Languages
    10→
    11→**Primary:**
    12→
    13→- TypeScript 5.4.x - All application code (PoC, planned web/desktop/backend)
    14→
    15→**Secondary:**
    16→
    17→- JavaScript (ES2022) - Compilation target
    18→- SQL - PostgreSQL database schema (planned)
    19→- Rust - TEE/Phala Cloud workloads (planned, referenced in spec)
    20→
    21→## Runtime
    22→
    23→**Environment:**
    24→
    25→- Node.js 20+ - PoC requires Node 20+
    26→- Browser (Chrome/Firefox/Safari) - Web app (planned)
    27→- Tauri/Electron - Desktop app (planned)
    28→
    29→**Package Manager:**
    30→
    31→- npm/yarn - `yarn.lock` present in PoC
    32→- Lockfile: present at `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/yarn.lock`
    33→
    34→## Frameworks
    35→
    36→**PoC (Current):**
    37→
    38→- No framework - Raw Node.js + TypeScript
    39→- `tsx` 4.7.1 - TypeScript execution
    40→
    41→**Planned Web App:**
    42→
    43→- React 18 - Frontend framework
    44→- Tailwind CSS - Styling
    45→- Axios - HTTP client
    46→
    47→**Planned Backend:**
    48→
    49→- NestJS - Backend framework (Node.js)
    50→- jose - JWT verification
    51→
    52→**Planned Desktop:**
    53→
    54→- Tauri (preferred) or Electron - Desktop shell
    55→- macFUSE/FUSE3/WinFSP - Filesystem mount
    56→
    57→**Build/Dev:**
    58→
    59→- TypeScript 5.4.2 - Type checking and compilation
    60→- ESLint 8.57.0 - Linting
    61→- tsx 4.7.1 - Dev execution
    62→
    63→## Key Dependencies
    64→
    65→**PoC Critical (from `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/package.json`):**
    66→
    67→- `ipfs-http-client` 60.0.1 - IPFS node communication
    68→- `eciesjs` 0.4.7 - ECIES encryption (secp256k1)
    69→- `@noble/secp256k1` 2.1.0 - Public key derivation
    70→- `dotenv` 16.4.5 - Environment configuration
    71→
    72→**PoC Dev Dependencies:**
    73→
    74→- `@types/node` 20.19.30 - Node.js type definitions
    75→- `typescript` 5.4.2 - TypeScript compiler
    76→- `tsx` 4.7.1 - TypeScript execution
    77→- `eslint` 8.57.0 - Linting
    78→
    79→**Planned Production:**
    80→
    81→- `@web3auth/modal` - Auth and key derivation
    82→- `jose` - JWT verification (backend)
    83→- PostgreSQL client - Database access (backend)
    84→- `winston` - Structured logging framework (backend)
    85→- `nest-winston` - NestJS Winston integration (backend)
    86→- Datadog/Splunk transport - Log aggregation for dev/prod environments
    87→
    88→## Configuration
    89→
    90→**Environment:**
    91→
    92→- `.env` file for local configuration
    93→- Environment variables for sensitive data
    94→- Key configs from `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/.env.example`:
    95→  - `ECDSA_PRIVATE_KEY` - Required, 32-byte hex
    96→  - `IPFS_API_URL` - IPFS daemon endpoint (default: <http://127.0.0.1:5001>)
    97→  - `IPFS_GATEWAY_URL` - IPFS gateway for reads
    98→  - `IPFS_LOCAL_API_URL`, `IPFS_LOCAL_GATEWAY_URL` - Kubo node endpoints
    99→  - `POC_STATE_DIR` - Local state persistence
   100→  - `IPNS_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`, `IPNS_POLL_TIMEOUT_MS` - Polling config
   101→
   102→**TypeScript (from `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/tsconfig.json`):**
   103→
   104→- Target: ES2022
   105→- Module: ES2022
   106→- ModuleResolution: Bundler
   107→- Strict mode enabled
   108→- Output: `dist/`
   109→
   110→**Build:**
   111→
   112→- `npm start` or `yarn start` - Run PoC via tsx
   113→- `npm run build` - Compile TypeScript
   114→- `npm run lint` - Run ESLint
   115→
   116→## Cryptography Stack
   117→
   118→**Symmetric Encryption:**
   119→
   120→- AES-256-GCM - File and metadata encryption (via Node.js `crypto`)
   121→- AES-256-CTR - Planned for streaming (v1.1+)
   122→
   123→**Asymmetric Encryption:**
   124→
   125→- ECIES (secp256k1) - Key wrapping via `eciesjs`
   126→- ECDSA (secp256k1) - Key derivation via Web3Auth (planned)
   127→- Ed25519 - IPNS record signing (planned, via `libsodium.js`)
   128→
   129→**Key Derivation:**
   130→
   131→- HKDF-SHA256 - Key derivation (planned)
   132→- SHA-256 - Hashing
   133→
   134→## Platform Requirements
   135→
   136→**Development:**
   137→

   140→- npm or yarn
   141→

   145→- IPFS Kubo node (pinning and storage)
   146→- Web3Auth project (auth)
   147→- TEE provider (Phala Cloud primary, AWS Nitro fallback)

```
     1→---
     2→created: 2026-02-14T00:00
     3→title: Add bring-your-own IPFS node support
     4→area: api
     5→files:
     6→  - apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.module.ts
     7→  - apps/api/src/ipfs/providers/ipfs-provider.interface.ts
     8→  - apps/api/src/ipfs/providers/local.provider.ts
     9→  - apps/api/src/ipfs/providers/kubo.provider.ts
    10→  - apps/api/.env.example
    11→  - apps/web/src/lib/api/ipfs.ts
    12→---
    13→
    14→## Problem
    15→
    16→Currently CipherBox uses a self-hosted Kubo node for IPFS, configured server-side via environment variables (`IPFS_LOCAL_API_URL`). There is no way for end users to bring their own IPFS node — the choice is made by the server operator, not the individual user.
    17→
    18→For a zero-knowledge privacy tool, users may want to:
    19→
    20→- Pin their encrypted data to their own IPFS node (self-sovereignty)
    21→- Use a preferred pinning service (e.g., web3.storage, Filebase, nft.storage)
    22→- Run a personal Kubo/IPFS node at home and have CipherBox pin directly to it
    23→- Avoid relying on a third-party pinning service entirely
    24→
    25→This requires a user-level IPFS configuration (not just server-level env vars) and potentially a hybrid model where the backend can relay uploads to a user-specified IPFS endpoint, or the client uploads directly to the user's node.
    26→
    27→## Solution
    28→
    29→TBD — key design questions to resolve:
    30→
    31→1. **Client-direct vs server-relay:** Should the web app upload encrypted blobs directly to the user's IPFS node (bypassing backend), or should the backend proxy to the user's configured endpoint?
    32→   - Client-direct: simpler, no server load, but CORS/connectivity issues
    33→   - Server-relay: consistent quota tracking, but server sees user's IPFS credentials
    34→
    35→2. **User settings model:** Add per-user IPFS config (endpoint URL, auth token, provider type) stored in user settings. UI for configuring custom node in settings page.
    36→
    37→3. **Provider abstraction:** The existing `IpfsProviderInterface` (pin/unpin/fetch) is already well-abstracted. A new "user-custom" provider that reads config from user settings rather than env vars would slot in cleanly.
    38→
    39→4. **IPNS implications:** If user pins to their own node, IPNS republishing and delegated routing need to work with the user's node or be handled differently.
    40→
    41→5. **Quota tracking:** If uploads go directly to user's node, server-side quota tracking becomes optional or advisory.
    42→
    43→6. **Fallback/reliability:** What happens if user's node is unreachable? Fallback to default provider, or fail?
    44→
    45→7. **Conflict detection:** Phase 16 (M2) adds API-level optimistic concurrency for conflict detection — the API checks IPNS sequence numbers on publish. BYO-IPFS bypasses the API for publishes, so this conflict detection mechanism will stop working. This feature will need a client-side conflict detection strategy (e.g., IPNS resolution before publish, or CRDT-based metadata) when BYO-IPFS is implemented.
    46→
```

{"url":"https://api.github.com/repos/FSM1/cipher-box/pulls/comments/2893124146","pull_request_review_id":3900506220,"id":2893124146,"node_id":"PRRC_kwDOQ6DXvM6scZIy","diff_hunk":"@@ -2,399 +2,109 @@\n   <img src=\"./cipherbox logo.png\" alt=\"CipherBox Logo\" width=\"450\"/>\n </p>\n \n-# CipherBox - README.md\n-\n-## Privacy-first cloud storage with decentralized persistence\n+<h3 align=\"center\">Privacy-first encrypted cloud storage with decentralized persistence</h3>\n \n ---\n \n-## 📄 Overview\n-\n-**CipherBox is a technology demonstrator** showcasing privacy-first cloud storage with decentralized persistence. It is **not intended as a commercial product** but as a proof-of-concept for:\n-\n-- **Zero-knowledge client-side encryption**\n-- **Decentralized storage via IPFS/IPNS**\n-- **Deterministic key derivation via Web3Auth**\n-- **Cross-device sync without server-side key access**\n-\n-**CipherBox** demonstrates:\n+## What is CipherBox\n \n-- **IPFS/IPNS** for decentralized, redundant storage\n-- **Web3Auth** for deterministic key derivation across multiple auth methods\n-- **AES-256-GCM + ECIES secp256k1** for layered E2E encryption\n-- **React web UI** + **FUSE desktop mount** (macOS v1)\n-- **Automatic multi-device sync** via IPNS polling\n-- **TEE-based IPNS republishing** via Phala Cloud / AWS Nitro (zero-knowledge)\n-\n----\n+CipherBox is a **technology demonstrator** for privacy-first cloud storage. All encryption happens client-side — the server never sees plaintext data, file names, or encryption keys. Files are stored on IPFS for decentralized persistence, keys are derived deterministically via Web3Auth so the same user always gets the same vault regardless of login method, and data can be exported for independent recovery without CipherBox.\n \n ## Acknowledgements\n \n This project is inspired by discussions and planning while working on [ChainSafe Files](https://github.com/chainsafe/ui-monorepo). A massive shout-out to all the colleagues I got to work with on the original ChainSafe Files project, who unknowingly contributed to this phoenix rising out of the ashes.\n \n----\n-\n-## 🎯 Vision\n-\n-**Replace Google Drive/Dropbox with:**\n-\n-```text\n-✓ Client-side encryption (server never sees plaintext)\n-✓ User-held keys (zero-knowledge guarantee)\n-✓ Decentralized storage (no vendor lock-in)\n-✓ Data portability (export vault, recover independently)\n-✓ Multi-device sync (automatic via IPFS)\n-✓ Transparent UX (hide IPFS complexity)\n-```\n-\n----\n-\n-## 📦 MVP Scope (v1.0)\n-\n-### ✅ **Included**\n-\n-```text\n-Auth: Email/Password, OAuth, Magic Link, External Wallet → Web3Auth key derivation\n-Storage: IPFS via Pinata (v1), per-folder IPNS entries\n-Encryption: AES-256-GCM files + ECIES key wrapping\n-Web UI: React file browser, drag-drop, folder ops\n-Desktop: macOS FUSE mount + background sync\n-Sync: IPNS polling (~30s eventual consistency)\n-TEE Republishing: Phala Cloud (primary) / AWS Nitro (fallback), every 3h\n-Portability: Vault export + independent recovery\n-```\n-\n-### ⏱️ **Deferred**\n-\n-```text\n-v1.1: Billing, Linux/Windows desktop, mobile apps\n-v2: File versioning, folder sharing, search\n-```\n-\n----\n-\n-## 🏗️ Technology Stack\n-\n-| Component          | Technology                    | Why                               |\n-| :----------------- | :---------------------------- | :-------------------------------- |\n-| **Frontend**       | React 18 + TypeScript         | Modern crypto UI                  |\n-| **Web Crypto**     | Web Crypto API                | Native browser encryption         |\n-| **Backend**        | Node.js + NestJS + TypeScript | Type-safe APIs                    |\n-| **Database**       | PostgreSQL                    | ACID audit trail                  |\n-| **Key Derivation** | Web3Auth Network              | Deterministic across auth methods |\n-| **Storage**        | IPFS via Pinata               | Redundant, decentralized          |\n-| **Desktop**        | Tauri/Electron + FUSE         | Transparent file access           |\n-| **TEE**            | Phala Cloud / AWS Nitro       | Zero-knowledge IPNS republishing  |\n-\n----\n-\n-## 🔐 Architecture Summary\n-\n-```text\n-User Device (Web/Desktop)\n-        ↓ Auth (4 methods)\n-CipherBox Backend (JWT)\n-        ↓\n-Web3Auth Network (Key Derivation)\n-        ↓ ECDSA Private Key (RAM only!)\n-User Device ← Vault Data ← PostgreSQL\n-        ↓ Encrypted Keys\n-IPFS (Pinata) ← Encrypted Files\n-        ↑\n-TEE (Phala/Nitro) ← IPNS Republish (every 3h)\n-```\n-\n-**Key Properties:**\n-\n-- Same user + any auth method → same keypair → same vault\n-- TEE republishes IPNS records even when all devices are offline\n-\n----\n-\n-## 🔑 Encryption Hierarchy\n-\n-CipherBox implements layered, zero-knowledge encryption where **all user data is encrypted client-side before leaving the device**. The server and storage layer never see plaintext — not file contents, not file names, not folder names, not timestamps, not file sizes.\n-\n-### Key Derivation\n-\n-Two paths produce the same `VaultKey` (a secp256k1 keypair):\n-\n-```text\n-┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n-│                       KEY DERIVATION                                │\n-├─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┤\n-│  Web3Auth (Social Login)│  External Wallet (MetaMask, etc.)        │\n-│                         │                                           │\n-│  Google/Email/etc.      │  1. Sign deterministic EIP-712 message   │\n-│        ↓                │  2. Normalize signature (low-S, EIP-2)   │\n-│  Web3Auth Network       │  3. HKDF-SHA256:                         │\n-│        ↓                │     salt  = \"CipherBox-ECIES-v1\"         │\n-│  secp256k1 keypair      │     info  = wallet address (lowercase)   │\n-│  (deterministic)        │     input = normalized signature          │\n-│                         │        ↓                                  │\n-│                         │  secp256k1 keypair (deterministic)       │\n-├─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┤\n-│                              ↓                                      │\n-│                   VaultKey (secp256k1)                               │\n-│          ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐                    │\n-│          │ Private Key: 32 bytes (RAM only!)   │                    │\n-│          │ Public Key:  65 bytes (uncompressed) │                   │\n-│          └─────────────────────────────────────┘                    │\n-└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n-```\n-\n-### Full Key Hierarchy\n-\n-Every key below the VaultKey is **randomly generated** (not derived) and **ECIES-wrapped** with the user's public key. Compromising one file key reveals nothing about other file keys.\n-\n-```text\n-    VaultKey (secp256k1 keypair)\n-    │\n-    │  ECIES-unwrap\n-    ├──────────────────────────────────────────┐\n-    │                                          │\n-    ▼                                          ▼\n- rootFolderKey (random 32B)          rootIpnsPrivateKey (Ed25519)\n-    │                                          │\n-    │  AES-256-GCM decrypt                     │  Signs IPNS records\n-    ▼                                          ▼\n- Root Folder Metadata (encrypted JSON)     IPNS publish/resolve\n-    │\n-    │  Contains per-child entries:\n-    │\n-    ├── File Entries ──────────────────────────────────────────┐\n-    │     name (encrypted)        ◄── only visible after       │\n-    │     size (encrypted)            decrypting metadata       │\n-    │     timestamps (encrypted)                                │\n-    │     fileKeyEncrypted ──── ECIES-unwrap ──► fileKey (32B) │\n-    │     fileIv (12B)                              │           │\n-    │     cid (IPFS ref)            AES-256-GCM decrypt        │\n-    │                                               ▼          │\n-    │                                        File Contents     │\n-    │                                                          │\n-    ├── Subfolder Entries ─────────────────────────────────────┤\n-    │     name (encrypted)        ◄── only visible after       │\n-    │     timestamps (encrypted)      decrypting metadata      │\n-    │     folderKeyEncrypted ── ECIES-unwrap ──► folderKey     │\n-    │     ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted ─ ECIES-unwrap ► ipnsKey     │\n-    │     ipnsName (k51...)                                    │\n-    │          │                                               │\n-    │          ▼                                               │\n-    │     Subfolder Metadata (same structure, recursive)       │\n-    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n-```\n-\n-### What's Encrypted vs. What's Visible\n+## Features\n \n-```text\n-┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n-│                    FULLY ENCRYPTED                               │\n-│   (requires user's private key + folder key to access)          │\n-│                                                                  │\n-│   ✓ File contents              ✓ File names                     │\n-│   ✓ Folder names               ✓ Folder structure / child list  │\n-│   ✓ File sizes                 ✓ Creation timestamps            │\n-│   ✓ Modification timestamps    ✓ All encryption keys            │\n-│   ✓ IPNS private keys          ✓ File-to-folder relationships   │\n-├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n-│                    VISIBLE (Plaintext)                           │\n-│   (required for IPFS/IPNS protocol operation)                   │\n-│                                                                  │\n-│   • IPFS CIDs (content-addressed hashes, no semantic meaning)  │\n-│   • IPNS names (k51... public identifiers for folders)         │\n-│   • Encrypted blob sizes (approximate original sizes)          │\n-│   • Encryption IVs (required for decryption, not secret)       │\n-│   • User's secp256k1 public key                                │\n-├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n-│                    NEVER STORED (RAM Only)                       │\n-│                                                                  │\n-│   • User's private key          • Decrypted file names          │\n-│   • Decrypted folder metadata   • Decrypted file contents       │\n-│   • Plaintext file/folder keys  • Wallet signatures             │\n-└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n-```\n-\n-### Cryptographic Primitives\n-\n-| Purpose | Algorithm | Parameters |\n-|:--|:--|:--|\n-| File & metadata encryption | AES-256-GCM | 256-bit key, 96-bit IV, 128-bit auth tag |\n-| Key wrapping | ECIES (secp256k1) | Ephemeral keypair + AES-GCM |\n-| Key derivation (wallets) | HKDF-SHA256 | 32-byte output, static salt |\n-| IPNS record signing | Ed25519 | 32-byte seed, 64-byte signatures |\n-| Random generation | `crypto.getRandomValues()` | CSPRNG (Web Crypto API) |\n-\n-### File Upload Flow\n-\n-```text\n-  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n-  │                    FILE UPLOAD FLOW                           │\n-  │                                                              │\n-  │  1. User selects \"document.pdf\"                              │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  2. Generate random fileKey (32 bytes)                       │\n-  │     Generate random IV (12 bytes)                            │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  3. AES-256-GCM encrypt(plaintext, fileKey, IV)              │\n-  │     → ciphertext ‖ auth_tag (16 bytes)                       │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  4. ECIES wrap(fileKey, userPublicKey)                        │\n-  │     → ephemeral_pubkey ‖ wrapped_key ‖ tag                   │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  5. Clear plaintext fileKey from memory                      │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  6. Upload encrypted blob → Pinata → IPFS → returns CID     │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  7. Add to folder metadata:                                  │\n-  │       { name, cid, fileKeyEncrypted, fileIv, size, ... }     │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  8. Re-encrypt folder metadata with folderKey (AES-256-GCM)  │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │  9. Upload encrypted metadata → IPFS → new CID              │\n-  │         │                                                    │\n-  │         ▼                                                    │\n-  │ 10. Publish IPNS record: /ipns/k51... → /ipfs/<new CID>     │\n-  │     (signed with folder's Ed25519 private key)               │\n-  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n-```\n-\n-### Defense in Depth\n-\n-Each file key is protected by multiple nested layers. An attacker must break through all layers to access any file content:\n-\n-```text\n-   File Content\n-     └─ encrypted with ──► fileKey (random, unique per file)\n-         └─ ECIES-wrapped with ──► User's Public Key\n-             └─ stored inside ──► Folder Metadata\n-                 └─ encrypted with ──► folderKey (random, unique per folder)\n-                     └─ ECIES-wrapped with ──► User's Public Key\n-                         └─ stored on ──► Server (zero-knowledge)\n-```\n-\n-### What an Attacker Sees\n-\n-With full access to IPFS and the CipherBox server but without the user's private key:\n-\n-```text\n-  IPFS (public network):\n-\n-    /ipfs/bafybei3a7x...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)\n-    /ipfs/bafybei9f2k...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)\n-    /ipfs/bafybeiqw8m...   ← encrypted blob (file? folder? unknown)\n-\n-  Without the user's private key:\n-    ✗ Cannot read file contents\n-    ✗ Cannot read file or folder names\n-    ✗ Cannot determine folder structure\n-    ✗ Cannot read timestamps or file sizes\n-    ✗ Cannot determine which blobs are files vs. folders\n-    ✓ Can see encrypted blob sizes (approximates original size)\n-    ✓ Can see IPNS update frequency (usage pattern)\n-```\n-\n----\n-\n-## 📊 6 Key Decisions\n-\n-### 1. **Web3Auth for Key Derivation**\n-\n-```text\n-Email/Password/OAuth/Magic Link/External Wallet → Web3Auth → Same ECDSA keypair\n-```\n+- **Authentication** — Web3Auth MPC Core Kit: email OTP, Google OAuth, magic link, external wallet. MFA via device factor. Same user identity = same vault.\n+- **Encryption** — Client-side AES-256-GCM for files and metadata. ECIES secp256k1 key wrapping (per-file, per-folder random keys). Streaming AES-256-CTR for large media files.\n+- **File Management** — Upload, download, rename, move, delete. Nested folders up to 20 levels. Drag-and-drop.\n+- **Sync** — Multi-device via IPNS polling (~30s). Conflict detection and resolution.\n+- **Desktop** — macOS via Tauri v2 + FUSE-T virtual filesystem mount. Background sync with system tray.\n+- **TEE Republishing** — Automatic IPNS record refresh every 6 hours via Phala Cloud. Zero-knowledge: keys decrypted only inside hardware enclaves.\n+- **Data Portability** — Full vault export (JSON + encrypted blobs). Standalone recovery with private key — no CipherBox required.\n \n-### 2. **Layered Encryption**\n+### Planned (v1.1+)\n \n-```text\n-File (AES-256-GCM) → Metadata (AES-256-GCM) → Keys (ECIES)\n-```\n+- **Sharing** — User-to-user sharing with ECIES re-wrapping. Link sharing with time-limited access tokens.\n+- **Search** — Client-side encrypted search index across file and folder names.\n+- **Versioning** — File history with point-in-time restore.\n+- **Recycle Bin** — 30-day soft-delete with restore.\n+- **Desktop (Windows & Linux)** — WinFSP / libfuse support.\n \n-### 3. **Per-Folder IPNS**\n+## Architecture Overview\n \n ```text\n-Root IPNS → Folder1 IPNS → Folder2 IPNS (modular sharing-ready)\n+┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────┐     ┌───────────────┐\n+│  Web / Desktop│────▶│  CipherBox   │────▶│  PostgreSQL   │\n+│   (Client)   │     │    API       │     │  (Metadata)   │\n+└──────┬───────┘     └──────┬───────┘     └───────────────┘\n+       │                    │\n+       │ Encrypted          │ Relay\n+       │ blobs              │\n+       ▼                    ▼\n+┌──────────────┐     ┌───────────────┐\n+│    IPFS      │     │  TEE Worker   │\n+│  (Storage)   │     │ (IPNS Refresh)│\n+└──────────────┘     └───────────────┘\n ```\n \n-### 4. **IPNS Polling Sync**\n+The client encrypts everything locally. The API is a zero-knowledge relay — it stores encrypted keys and routes IPFS/IPNS operations but never accesses plaintext. For full cryptographic details, see [docs/ARCHITECTURE.md](docs/ARCHITECTURE.md).\n \n-```text\n-30s polling, no push infrastructure (MVP simple)\n-```\n+## Tech Stack\n \n-### 5. **Zero-Knowledge Keys**\n+| Component          | Technology                                    |\n+| :----------------- | :-------------------------------------------- |\n+| **Frontend**       | React 18 + TypeScript + Tailwind CSS          |\n+| **Backend**        | Node.js + NestJS + TypeScript                 |\n+| **Database**       | PostgreSQL 16                                 |\n+| **Job Queue**      | BullMQ + Redis                                |\n+| **Key Derivation** | Web3Auth MPC Core Kit                         |\n+| **Storage**        | IPFS via Kubo                                 |\n+| **Desktop**        | Tauri v2 + FUSE-T / WinFSP                    |\n+| **TEE**            | Phala Cloud (IPNS republishing)               |\n+| **Crypto**         | Web Crypto API (AES-256-GCM, ECIES secp256k1) |\n \n-```text\n-Server holds: Encrypted root key only\n-Client holds: Private key (RAM only)\n-```\n-\n-### 6. **TEE-Based IPNS Republishing**\n+## Project Structure\n \n ```text\n-IPNS records expire after ~24h → TEE republishes every 3h\n-Client encrypts ipnsPrivateKey with TEE public key (ECIES)\n-TEE decrypts in hardware, signs, zeroes key immediately\n-Providers: Phala Cloud (primary) / AWS Nitro (fallback)\n+cipher-box/\n+├── apps/\n+│   ├── api/              # NestJS backend\n+│   ├── web/              # React frontend\n+│   └── desktop/          # Tauri v2 desktop app\n+├── packages/\n+│   ├── crypto/           # Shared encryption library\n+│   └── api-client/       # OpenAPI spec; generated typed client lives in apps/web/src/api/\n+├── tee-worker/           # Phala Cloud TEE worker\n+├── tests/\n+│   ├── e2e/              # Playwright E2E tests\n+│   └── e2e-desktop/      # Desktop E2E tests\n+└── docker/               # Docker Compose (PostgreSQL, IPFS, Redis)\n ```\n \n----\n-\n-## 🛤️ User Journey (Example)\n+## Getting Started\n \n-```text\n-1. Signup (Google) → Web3Auth derives KeyA\n-2. Upload file → Encrypt → IPFS CID → IPNS publish\n-3. Phone login (Email) → Web3Auth derives KeyA (same!)\n-4. Phone polls IPNS → Sees file → Downloads & decrypts\n-5. Export vault → JSON with CIDs + encrypted root key\n-6. CipherBox gone? → Use export + private key → Full recovery\n+```bash\n+docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml up -d\n+pnpm install\n+pnpm dev   # starts API (:3000) + web (:5173)\n ```\n \n----\n+See [docs/DEVELOPMENT.md](docs/DEVELOPMENT.md) for full setup instructions including desktop app, testing, and environment configuration.\n \n-## 🔐 Security\n+## Documentation\n \n-```text\n-✅ Zero-Knowledge: Private keys never on server\n-✅ E2E Encryption: AES-256-GCM + ECIES secp256k1\n-✅ TEE Republishing: IPNS keys decrypted only in hardware enclaves\n-✅ Data Portability: Export vault, recover independently\n-✅ No Tracking: No analytics/telemetry\n-✅ Threat Model: See TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md\n-```\n-\n----\n-\n-## 📋 Success Criteria (Tech Demo)\n-\n-| Criterion             | Target                                                    |\n-| :-------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------- |\n-| **Privacy**           | Private keys never on server (cryptographically enforced) |\n-| **Encryption**        | AES-256-GCM + ECIES correctly implemented                 |\n-| **Key Derivation**    | Same user + any auth method → same keypair                |\n-| **Multi-Device Sync** | <30s via IPNS polling                                     |\n-| **Data Recovery**     | Vault export enables independent recovery                 |\n-| **Zero Dependencies** | Can decrypt vault without CipherBox service               |\n+| Document                                                                   | Description                                                  |\n+| :------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------------- |\n+|  |\n+| :---------------- | :------------------------- | :-------------------------------- |\n+| PostgreSQL 16     | 5432                       | Database                          |\n+| IPFS (Kubo)       | 5001 (API), 8080 (Gateway) | Decentralized storage             |\n+| Redis 7           | 6380                       | BullMQ job queue                  |\n+| Mock IPNS Routing | 3001                       | Local IPNS resolution for dev/E2E |\n+\n+## Environment\n+\n+Copy the example env files:\n+\n+```bash\n+cp apps/api/.env.example apps/api/.env\n+cp apps/web/.env.example           | Redis host                              |\n+| `REDIS_PORT`           | `6380`                  | Redis port (mapped from container 6379) |\n+| `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` |    115→- TEE: Trusted for IPNS republishing only (hardware isolation)
   116→- IPFS: Untrusted (only sees ciphertext)
   117→
   118→**Key Hierarchy:**
   119→
   120→```text
   121→User Private Key (secp256k1, from Web3Auth)
   122→    └── Root Folder Key (derived, AES-256)
   123→        └── Folder Keys (per-folder, AES-256)
   124→            └── File Keys (per-file, AES-256)
   125→```
   126→
   127→**Critical Rules:**
   128→
   129→- ECIES for key wrapping
   130→- AES-256-GCM for content encryption
   131→- Web Crypto API only (no JS crypto libraries)
   132→- Uint8Array for all binary data
   133→- Never expose keys to server
   134→- TEE receives only encrypted IPNS keys
```

     1→# External Integrations
     2→
     3→**Analysis Date:** 2026-01-20
     4→
     5→## Project Status
     6→
     7→CipherBox is a **technology demonstrator** with integrations split between:
     8→
     9→- **Implemented (PoC)**: Local IPFS daemon
    10→- **Planned (Production)**: Web3Auth, PostgreSQL, IPFS (Kubo), TEE providers
    11→
    12→## APIs & External Services
    13→
    14→**IPFS/IPNS (Implemented in PoC):**
    15→
    16→- Local IPFS daemon (Kubo) - File storage and IPNS publishing
    17→  - SDK/Client: `ipfs-http-client` 60.0.1
    18→  - Connection: `IPFS_API_URL` env var (default: <http://127.0.0.1:5001>)
    19→  - Gateway: `IPFS_GATEWAY_URL` env var (optional)
    20→  - Usage: `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/src/index.ts`
    21→
    22→**Web3Auth (Planned - Not Implemented):**
    23→
    24→- Authentication and key derivation - User identity
    25→  - SDK/Client: `@web3auth/modal` (planned)
    26→  - Auth methods: Google, Apple, GitHub, Email, Magic Link, External Wallet
    27→  - JWKS endpoint: `https://api-auth.web3auth.io/jwks`
    28→  - Key feature: Group connections for deterministic keypair derivation
    29→  - Spec: `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md` Section 2
    30→
    31→**TEE Providers (Implemented — `tee-worker/`):**
    32→
    33→- Trusted Execution Environment for IPNS republishing
    34→
    35→**Phala Cloud (Primary):**
    36→
    37→- TEE-based IPNS key decryption and signing
    38→  - Cost: ~$0.10/hr
    39→  - Features: Intel SGX hardware attestation, on-chain verification
    40→  - Latency: 12-30s per republish
    41→  - Spec: `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md` Section 9
    42→
    43→**AWS Nitro Enclaves (Fallback):**
    44→
    45→- Backup TEE provider
    46→  - Cost: ~$0.17-0.50/hr
    47→  - Features: AWS custom silicon, AWS attestation API
    48→  - Latency: <100ms per republish
    49→
    50→## Data Storage
