{"url":"https://api.github.com/repos/FSM1/cipher-box/pulls/comments/2893194715","pull_request_review_id":3900589073,"id":2893194715,"node_id":"PRRC_kwDOQ6DXvM6scqXb","diff_hunk":"@@ -0,0 +1,280 @@\n+# Architecture\n+\n+CipherBox implements layered, zero-knowledge encryption where **all user data is encrypted client-side before leaving the device**. The server and storage layer never see plaintext — not file contents, not file names, not folder names, not timestamps, not plaintext file sizes (encrypted blob sizes are visible for storage accounting).\n+\n+## System Overview\n+\n+```text\n+User Device (Web/Desktop)\n+        ↓ Auth (4 methods)\n+CipherBox Backend (JWT)\n+        ↓\n+Web3Auth Network (Key Derivation)\n+        ↓ ECDSA Private Key (RAM only!)\n+User Device ← Vault Data ← PostgreSQL\n+        ↓ Encrypted Keys\n+IPFS (Kubo) ← Encrypted Files\n+        ↑\n+TEE (Phala; Nitro fallback planned) ← IPNS Republish (every 6h)\n+```\n+\n+**Key Properties:**\n+\n+- Same user identity → same keypair → same vault (auth methods share a vault when they resolve to the same CipherBox `userId`; see [Authentication Architecture](AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md))\n+- TEE republishes IPNS records even when all devices are offline\n+\n+## Key Derivation\n+\n+Two paths produce the same `VaultKey` (a secp256k1 keypair):\n+\n+```text\n+┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n+│                       KEY DERIVATION                                │\n+├─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┤\n+│  Web3Auth (Social Login)│  Web3Auth (External Wallet via SIWE)     │\n+│                         │                                           │\n+│  Google/Email/etc.      │  1. Wallet signs SIWE (EIP-4361) message │\n+│        ↓                │  2. Backend verifies SIWE, issues JWT    │\n+│  Web3Auth Network       │  3. Web3Auth Core Kit validates JWT      │\n+│        ↓                │  4. Web3Auth Network                     │\n+│  secp256k1 keypair      │        ↓                                  │\n+│  (deterministic)        │  secp256k1 keypair (deterministic)       │\n+│                         │                                           │\n+│                         │                                           │\n+├─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┤\n+│                              ↓                                      │\n+│                   VaultKey (secp256k1)                               │\n+│          ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐                    │\n+│          │ Private Key: 32 bytes (RAM only!)   │                    │\n+│          │ Public Key:  65 bytes (uncompressed) │                   │\n+│          └─────────────────────────────────────┘                    │\n+└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n+```\n+\n+## Encryption Hierarchy\n+\n+Keys below the VaultKey are either **randomly generated** or **HKDF-derived**, then **ECIES-wrapped** with the user's public key. IPNS keypairs (vault, device-registry, per-file) are derived deterministically via HKDF-SHA256 from the user's private key (see [Cryptographic Primitives](#cryptographic-primitives)). Content encryption keys (rootFolderKey, per-folder keys, per-file keys) are randomly generated. Compromising one file key reveals nothing about other file keys.\n+\n+```text\n+    VaultKey (secp256k1 keypair)\n+    │\n+    │  ECIES-unwrap\n+    ├──────────────────────────────────────────┐\n+    │                                          │\n+    ▼                                          ▼\n+ rootFolderKey (random 32B)          rootIpnsPrivateKey (Ed25519, HKDF-derived)\n+    │                                          │\n+    │  AES-256-GCM decrypt                     │  Signs IPNS records\n+    ▼                                          ▼\n+ Root Folder Metadata (encrypted JSON)     IPNS publish/resolve\n+    │\n+    │  Contains per-child entries:\n+    │\n+    ├── File Pointer Entries ────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n+    │     name (encrypted)           ◄── only visible after              │\n+    │     timestamps (encrypted)         decrypting metadata             │\n+    │     ipnsName (k51...)  ────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n+    │          │                                                        │\n+    │          ▼                                                        │\n+    │     File Metadata (per-file IPNS record, encrypted JSON)          │\n+    │         fileKeyEncrypted ─ ECIES-unwrap ──► fileKey (32B)         │","path":"docs/ARCHITECTURE.md","commit_id":"0fba1dab498e587878663f3c20662d450c7b471d","original_commit_id":"a6618f22cb15c2e462a2b3024f9294ae8481e763","user":{"login":"FSM1","id":12774278,"node_id":"MDQ6VXNlcjEyNzc0Mjc4","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/12774278?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1","html_url":"https://github.com/FSM1","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/received_events","type":"User","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false},"body":"Fixed     10→All files in `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/` are **FINALIZED** specifications (version 1.11.1, status: Finalized). These documents represent the agreed-upon design and should **NOT** be modified.
    11→
    12→**If you need to make changes:**
    13→
    14→- New implementation documentation should be created in a separate location
    15→- Working notes and updates belong in `.planning/` or project-specific directories
    16→- Do not modify version numbers or content in `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/`
    17→
    18→---
    19→
    20→## Project Overview
    21→
    22→**CipherBox** is a **technology demonstrator** for privacy-first, zero-knowledge encrypted cloud storage using IPFS/IPNS for decentralized persistence and Web3Auth for deterministic key derivation.
    23→
    24→**Purpose:** This is not a commercial product. It demonstrates novel applications of cryptography and decentralized systems.
    25→
    26→**Core Principle:** The server NEVER sees plaintext data or unencrypted keys. All encryption/decryption happens client-side.
    27→
    28→## Documentation Structure
    29→
    30→| Document                                                                                   | Purpose                                    |
    31→| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------ |
    32→| [PRD.md](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/PRD.md)                                       | Product requirements, user journeys, scope |
    33→| [TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md) | Encryption, key hierarchy, system design   |
    34→| [API_SPECIFICATION.md](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/API_SPECIFICATION.md)           | Backend endpoints, database schema         |
    35→| [DATA_FLOWS.md](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/DATA_FLOWS.md)                         | Sequence diagrams, test vectors            |
    36→| [CLIENT_SPECIFICATION.md](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/CLIENT_SPECIFICATION.md)     | Web UI, desktop app specs                  |
    37→
    38→---
    39→
    40→## Critical Architecture Patterns
    41→
    42→### Two-Phase Authentication Model
    43→
    44→CipherBox uses a **mandatory two-phase auth flow** that must be understood before implementing any auth-related features:
    45→
    46→1. **Phase 1 (Web3Auth):** User authenticates → Web3Auth derives ECDSA secp256k1 keypair via threshold cryptography
    47→2. **Phase 2 (CipherBox Backend):** Client authenticates with backend using either:
    48→   - **Option A:** Web3Auth ID token (JWT) validated via JWKS endpoint
    49→   - **Option B:** SIWE-like signature flow (nonce-based)
    50→
    51→**Key Insight:** The ECDSA keypair from Phase 1 is the user's identity. Web3Auth's "group connections" feature ensures that Google OAuth, email/password, magic links, and external wallet auth all derive the **same keypair** for the same user.
    52→
    53→### Zero-Knowledge Key Hierarchy
    54→
    55→```text
    56→User Auth → Web3Auth → ECDSA Private Key (client RAM only, never transmitted)
    57→                    ├─ Used for ECIES decryption of all data keys
    58→                    ├─ Used to decrypt IPNS signing keys (Ed25519)
    59→                    └─ Destroyed on logout
    60→
    61→ECDSA Public Key (stored on server, identifies user)
    62→    └─ Used to encrypt (ECIES):
    63→        ├─ Root Folder Key (AES-256, stored encrypted on server)
    64→        ├─ Subfolder Keys (AES-256, stored encrypted in parent IPNS metadata)
    65→        └─ File Keys (AES-256, stored encrypted in folder IPNS metadata)
    66→```
    67→
    68→**Critical Rule:** Never log, persist to disk, or transmit the ECDSA private key. It exists ONLY in client memory during the session.
    69→
    70→### Per-Folder IPNS Architecture
    71→
    72→Each folder has its own IPNS entry containing encrypted metadata. This design enables future per-folder sharing (v2+).
    73→
    74→**Folder Metadata Structure:**
    75→
    76→```typescript
    77→interface DecryptedFolderMetadata {
    78→  children: Array<{
    79→    type: 'file' | 'folder';
    80→    nameEncrypted: string; // AES-256-GCM(name, folderKey)
    81→    nameIv: string;
    82→    cid?: string; // For files: IPFS CID
    83→    ipnsName?: string; // For folders: IPNS name
    84→    fileKeyEncrypted?: string; // ECIES(fileKey, userPubkey)
    85→    subfolderKeyEncrypted?: string; // ECIES(folderKey, userPubkey)
    86→    size?: number;
    87→    created: number;
    88→    modified: number;
    89→  }>;
    90→}
    91→```
    92→
    93→**When modifying files/folders:** Always re-encrypt and republish the containing folder's IPNS entry.
    94→
    95→## Technology Stack & Standards
    96→
    97→### Encryption Primitives (Non-Negotiable)
    98→
    99→| Algorithm           | Use Case                            | Implementation                |
   100→| ------------------- | ----------------------------------- | ----------------------------- |
   101→| **AES-256-GCM**     | File content + metadata encryption  | Web Crypto API / libsodium.js |
   102→| **ECIES secp256k1** | Key wrapping (files, folders)       | ethers.js / libsodium.js      |
   103→| **ECDSA secp256k1** | IPNS signing, user identity         | Web3Auth / ethers.js          |
   104→| **HKDF-SHA256**     | Key derivation                      | Web Crypto API                |
   105→| **Argon2**          | Password hashing (server-side only) | argon2-browser                |
   106→
   107→**Never use:** Custom crypto, CBC mode, ECB mode, MD5, SHA1
   108→
   109→### Target Tech Stack (v1.0)
   110→
   111→- **Frontend:** React 18 + TypeScript + Tailwind CSS
   112→- **Backend:** Node.js + NestJS + TypeScript
   113→- **Database:** PostgreSQL (users, vaults, audit trail)
   114→- **Storage:** IPFS via Kubo
   115→- **Auth:** Web3Auth Modal SDK (@web3auth/modal)
   116→- **Desktop:** Tauri/Electron + FUSE (macFUSE/FUSE3/WinFSP)
   117→
   118→## Development Patterns
   119→
   120→### API Development Workflow
   121→
   122→When working on `apps/api` code:
   123→
   124→1. **After modifying API endpoints, DTOs, or controllers**, regenerate the API client to keep the web app in sync:
   125→
   126→   ```bash
   127→   pnpm api:generate
   128→   ```
   129→
   130→   This command generates the OpenAPI spec from the API, creates the typed client for the web app, and runs lint fixes.
   131→
   132→2. **Always run `pnpm api:generate` before completing a feature** that touches the API to ensure type safety across the monorepo.
   133→
   134→3. **Commit the regenerated client files** (`apps/web/src/api/`) along with your API changes.
   135→
   136→### File Upload Flow (Reference Implementation)
   137→
   138→```typescript
   139→// 1. Generate random file key
   140→const fileKey = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(32));
   141→const fileIV = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(12)); // GCM uses 96-bit IV
   142→
   143→// 2. Encrypt file content
   144→const encryptedFile = await crypto.subtle.encrypt(
   145→  { name: 'AES-GCM', iv: fileIV },
   146→  await crypto.subtle.importKey('raw', fileKey, 'AES-GCM', false, ['encrypt']),
   147→  fileContent
   148→);
   149→
   150→// 3. Wrap file key with user's public key (ECIES)
   151→const encryptedFileKey = await eciesEncrypt(fileKey, userPublicKey);
   152→
   153→// 4. Upload encrypted file to backend → IPFS → get CID
   154→// Endpoint: POST /ipfs/upload (multipart/form-data with 'file' field)
   155→const formData = new FormData();
   156→formData.append('file', new Blob([encryptedFile]));
   157→const { cid } = await api.post('/ipfs/upload', formData);
   158→// NOTE: No fileName sent — the server never sees plaintext file names
   159→
   160→// 5. Add to folder metadata (all encrypted)
   161→const fileEntry = {
   162→  type: 'file',
   163→  nameEncrypted: await aesEncrypt(file.name, folderKey),
   164→  nameIv: crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(12)),
   165→  cid,
   166→  fileKeyEncrypted: encryptedFileKey,
   167→  fileIv: bytesToHex(fileIV),
   168→  size: file.size,
   169→  created: Date.now(),
   170→  modified: Date.now(),
   171→};
   172→
   173→// 6. Re-encrypt folder metadata and republish IPNS
   174→await republishFolderIPNS(folderId, updatedMetadata);
   175→```
   176→
   177→### IPNS Publishing Pattern
   178→
   179→Every write operation (create/rename/move/delete) must:
   180→
   181→1. Update the in-memory folder metadata
   182→2. Re-encrypt the entire metadata JSON with the folder key
   183→3. Sign the encrypted metadata with ECDSA private key
   184→4. Call `POST /vault/publish-ipns` with signed entry
   185→
   186→**Performance Note:** IPNS publishing is the bottleneck (~2s). Batch operations when possible (e.g., multi-file upload should publish once at the end).
   187→
   188→### Session Token Management
   189→
   190→```typescript
   191→// Access tokens (15min, memory only)
   192→const accessToken = response.data.accessToken;
   193→// Store in: React context / Zustand / Redux (never localStorage)
   194→
   195→// Refresh tokens (7 days, secure storage)
   196→const refreshToken = response.data.refreshToken;
   197→// Store in: HTTP-only cookie (preferred) OR encrypted localStorage
   198→
   199→// Token refresh pattern
   200→if (error.response?.status === 401) {
   201→  const newAccessToken = await refreshAccessToken();
   202→  // Retry original request with new token
   203→}
   204→```
   205→
   206→## Common Pitfalls & Anti-Patterns
   207→
   208→### ❌ Never Do This
   209→
   210→1. **Storing keys in plaintext localStorage**
   211→
   212→   ```typescript
   213→   // WRONG
   214→   localStorage.setItem('privateKey', privateKeyHex);
   215→   ```
   216→
   217→2. **Logging sensitive data**
   218→
   219→   ```typescript
   220→   // WRONG
   221→   console.log('User private key:', privateKey);
   222→   console.log('Decrypted file:', fileContent);
   223→   ```
   224→
   225→3. **Sending plaintext to server**
   226→
   227→   ```typescript
   228→   // WRONG
   229→   await api.post('/vault/upload', { fileContent: file });
   230→   ```
   231→
   232→4. **Using sync crypto in main thread**
   233→
   234→   ```typescript
   235→   // WRONG (blocks UI for large files)
   236→   const encrypted = aesEncryptSync(largeFile, key);
   237→   ```
   238→
   239→### ✅ Correct Patterns
   240→
   241→1. **Store keys in memory, clear on logout**
   242→
   243→   ```typescript
   244→   // React context for session-scoped state
   245→   const [ecdsaPrivateKey, setEcdsaPrivateKey] = useState<Uint8Array | null>(null);
   246→
   247→   const logout = () => {
   248→     setEcdsaPrivateKey(null); // Cleared from memory
   249→     // No disk persistence
   250→   };
   251→   ```
   252→
   253→2. **Use Web Workers for large file encryption**
   254→
   255→   ```typescript
   256→   const worker = new Worker('./crypto-worker.js');
   257→   worker.postMessage({ fileData, key });
   258→   worker.onmessage = (e) => {
   259→     const encrypted = e.data;
   260→   };
   261→   ```
   262→
   263→3. **Always validate IPNS signatures client-side**
   264→
   265→   ```typescript
   266→   const messageHash = await sha256(signedEntry.encryptedMetadata);
   267→   const isValid = await ecdsaVerify(messageHash, signedEntry.signature, userPubkey);
   268→   if (!isValid) throw new Error('Metadata tampering detected');
   269→   ```
   270→
   271→## MVP Scope Boundaries (v1.0)
   272→
   273→### ✅ In Scope
   274→
   275→- Multi-method auth (email/pass, OAuth, magic link, external wallet)
   276→- File upload/download (E2E encrypted)
   277→- Folder operations (create/rename/move/delete)
   278→- Web UI (React)
   279→- Desktop mount (macOS FUSE only for v1.0)
   280→- Multi-device sync (IPNS polling, ~30s latency)
   281→- Vault export (data portability)
   282→
   283→### ❌ Out of Scope (defer to v1.1+)
   284→
   285→- Billing/payments
   286→- File versioning
   287→- Folder sharing
   288→- Search/indexing
   289→- Mobile apps
   290→- Linux/Windows desktop (v1.1)
   291→
   292→**When users request out-of-scope features:** Acknowledge the value, but clarify they're post-MVP. Suggest workarounds if applicable.
   293→
   294→## Testing Requirements
   295→
   296→### Security Test Checklist
   297→
   298→- [ ] Private key never logged (search logs for "privateKey", "ecdsa", "0x04")
   299→- [ ] Private key never in localStorage/sessionStorage
   300→- [ ] All file uploads: server receives ciphertext only
   301→- [ ] All folder names: encrypted in IPNS metadata
   302→- [ ] IPNS signature verification passes
   303→- [ ] Wrong private key cannot decrypt vault
   304→
   305→### Performance Benchmarks (P95)
   306→
   307→- Auth flow: <3s
   308→- File upload (<100MB): <5s
   309→- File download (<100MB): <5s
   310→- IPNS publish: <2s
   311→- Folder create: <1s
   312→- Multi-device sync latency: <30s
   313→

   321→- [00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/API_SPECIFICATION.md](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/API_SPECIFICATION.md) - Backend endpoints, database schema
   322→- [00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/DATA_FLOWS.md](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/DATA_FLOWS.md) - Sequence diagrams, test vectors

   324→
   325→**For detailed crypto flows:** See TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md (key hierarchy, encryption primitives) and DATA_FLOWS.md (sequence diagrams, test vectors)
   326→
   327→## API Conventions (When Backend Exists)
   328→
   329→### Expected Endpoints
   330→
   331→- `POST /auth/login` - Authenticate with Web3Auth ID token or SIWE signature
   332→- `GET /auth/nonce` - Get nonce for SIWE flow
   333→- `POST /auth/refresh` - Refresh access token
   334→- `GET /my-vault` - Get encrypted root key + IPNS name
   335→- `POST /my-vault/initialize` - Create vault (first-time setup)
   336→- `POST /vault/upload` - Upload encrypted file → IPFS → return CID
   337→- `POST /vault/publish-ipns` - Publish signed IPNS entry
   338→- `POST /vault/export` - Generate vault export JSON
   339→
   340→### Response Patterns
   341→
   342→```typescript
   343→// Success
   344→{ success: true, data: { cid: "QmXxxx..." } }
   345→
   346→// Error
   347→{ success: false, error: { code: "VAULT_NOT_INITIALIZED", message: "..." } }
   348→```
   349→
   350→## Web3Auth Integration Notes
   351→
   352→### Group Connections Setup (Dashboard)
   353→
   354→Configure in Web3Auth dashboard to link multiple auth methods:
   355→
   356→```text
   357→cipherbox-aggregate (group ID)
   358→  ├─ google (Google OAuth)
   359→  ├─ email_passwordless (Magic Link)
   360→  ├─ auth0 (Email/Password via Auth0)
   361→  └─ external_wallets (MetaMask, WalletConnect)
   362→```
   363→
   364→### Client Integration
   365→
   366→```typescript
   367→import { Web3Auth } from '@web3auth/modal';
   368→
   369→const web3auth = new Web3Auth({
   370→  clientId: 'YOUR_CLIENT_ID',
   371→  web3AuthNetwork: 'sapphire_mainnet', // or testnet
   372→  chainConfig: {
   373→    chainNamespace: 'eip155',
   374→    chainId: '0x1', // Ethereum mainnet (or any chain)
   375→  },
   376→});
   377→
   378→await web3auth.initModal();
   379→const web3authProvider = await web3auth.connect();
   380→
   381→// Get private key (use sparingly, clear from memory ASAP)
   382→const privateKey = await web3authProvider.request({
   383→  method: 'eth_private_key',
   384→});
   385→
   386→// Get ID token for CipherBox backend auth
   387→const { idToken } = await web3auth.authenticateUser();
   388→```
   389→
   390→## Verification Requirements
   391→
   392→### Playwright MCP for Application Testing
   393→
   394→**ALWAYS attempt to verify application changes using Playwright MCP** when it is available. This ensures implemented features work correctly at runtime.
   395→
   396→**Verification workflow:**
   397→
   398→1. Navigate to the app URL (typically `http://localhost:5173`)
   399→2. Wait for page elements to load
   400→3. Capture screenshots for visual verification
   401→4. Verify element existence and content
   402→5. Test interactive features (clicks, form submissions)
   403→6. Verify computed styles match design specifications
   404→
   405→**If Playwright MCP is not available:**
   406→
   407→- Document what needs manual verification
   408→- Provide step-by-step test instructions
   409→- Flag items that require human review
   410→
   411→### Pencil Design Verification
   412→
   413→For UI work, verify implementations against Pencil design files:
   414→
   415→1. **Design files are at:** `designs/*.pen` (JSON format)
   416→2. **Extract exact values:** Colors (hex), spacing (pixels), typography
   417→3. **Verify CSS matches:** Check both source CSS and computed styles
   418→4. **Document discrepancies:** Include file/line references for fixes
   419→
   420→**Key design specs:**
   421→
   422→- Colors: `#000000` (background), `#00D084` (primary green)
   423→- Font: JetBrains Mono (400, 600, 700 weights)
   424→- Spacing: 8px, 12px, 16px, 24px, 32px scale
   425→
   426→## Questions to Ask When Unclear
   427→
   428→1. **Auth-related:** "Does this require Phase 1 (Web3Auth) or Phase 2 (CipherBox backend) authentication?"
   429→2. **Encryption:** "Should this data be encrypted client-side before transmission?"
   430→3. **Keys:** "Which key in the hierarchy (ECDSA private, root folder, subfolder, file) should be used here?"
   431→4. **IPNS:** "Does this operation require republishing an IPNS entry?"
   432→5. **Scope:** "Is this feature in v1.0 MVP scope, or should it be deferred to v1.1+?"
   433→
   434→## Final Note
   435→
   436→This project prioritizes **cryptographic correctness over convenience**. When in doubt, err on the side of more encryption, more validation, and stricter security. The target user (cypherpunks, crypto enthusiasts) values privacy guarantees more than UX polish.
   437→
   438→**For detailed guidance:** Refer to [PRD.md](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/PRD.md) for product scope, [TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md) for crypto and system design, and [DATA_FLOWS.md](../00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/DATA_FLOWS.md) for test vectors and sequence diagrams.
   439→

     1→# CipherBox - Claude AI Rules
     2→
     3→## Project Context
     4→
     5→CipherBox is a **technology demonstrator** for privacy-first encrypted cloud storage using IPFS/IPNS and Web3Auth. It is not a commercial product.
     6→
     7→## Documentation Structure
     8→
     9→| Document                                                     | Purpose                                    |
    10→| ------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------ |
    11→| `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/PRD.md`                    | Product requirements, user journeys, scope |
    12→| `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md` | Encryption, key hierarchy, system design   |
    13→| `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/API_SPECIFICATION.md`      | Backend endpoints, database schema         |
    14→| `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/DATA_FLOWS.md`             | Sequence diagrams, test vectors            |
    15→| `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/CLIENT_SPECIFICATION.md`   | Web UI, desktop app specs                  |
    16→| `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP.md` | Week-by-week development plan              |
    17→
    18→## Finalized Specifications
    19→
    20→**Current Version:** 1.11.1
    21→**Status:** Finalized (2026-01-20)
    22→
    23→### ⚠️ IMPORTANT: Do Not Edit Preliminary/Documentation Files
    24→
    25→All files in `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/` are **FINALIZED** specifications (version 1.10.0, status: Finalized). These documents represent the agreed-upon design and should **NOT** be modified.
    26→**If you need to make changes:**
    27→
    28→- New implementation documentation should be created in a separate location
    29→- Working notes and updates belong in `.planning/` or project-specific directories
    30→- Do not modify version numbers or content in `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/` unless explicityly authorized.
    31→
    32→## Terminology Standards
    33→
    34→Always use consistent terminology:
    35→
    36→| Correct                   | Avoid                                              |
    37→| ------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
    38→| `publicKey`               | `pubkey`, `user_pubkey`, `ownerPublicKey`          |
    39→| `privateKey`              | `privkey`, `user_private_key`                      |
    40→| `rootFolderKey`           | `rootKey`, `root_folder_key`                       |
    41→| `ipnsName`                | IPNS entry (for identifier)                        |
    42→| `ipnsRecord`              | IPNS entry (for data structure)                    |
    43→| `folderKey`               | `subfolderKey` (unless specifically for subfolder) |
    44→| `fileKey`                 | `file_key`                                         |
    45→| `keyEpoch`                | `epoch`, `key_epoch`                               |
    46→| `encryptedIpnsPrivateKey` | `encrypted_ipns_key`, `ipns_key_encrypted`         |
    47→| `teePublicKey`            | `tee_pubkey`, `TEE_public_key`                     |
    48→
    49→## Critical Security Rules
    50→
    51→1. **Never** suggest storing `privateKey` in localStorage/sessionStorage
    52→2. **Never** suggest logging sensitive keys
    53→3. **Never** suggest sending unencrypted keys to server
    54→4. **Always** use ECIES for key wrapping
    55→5. **Always** use AES-256-GCM for content encryption
    56→6. The server NEVER has access to plaintext or unencrypted keys
    57→7. **Always** encrypt `ipnsPrivateKey` with TEE public key before sending for republishing
    58→8. TEE decrypts IPNS keys in hardware only, signs, and immediately discards
    59→
    60→## API Development Workflow
    61→
    62→When working on `apps/api` code:
    63→
    64→1. **After modifying API endpoints, DTOs, or controllers**, regenerate the API client to keep the web app in sync:
    65→
    66→   ```bash
    67→   pnpm api:generate
    68→   ```
    69→
    70→   This command generates the OpenAPI spec from the API, creates the typed client for the web app, and runs lint fixes.
    71→
    72→2. **Always run `pnpm api:generate` before completing a feature** that touches the API to ensure type safety across the monorepo.
    73→
    74→3. **Commit the regenerated client files** (`apps/web/src/api/`) along with your API changes.
    75→
    76→## Code Generation Guidelines
    77→
    78→When generating code for CipherBox:
    79→
    80→1. Use TypeScript for all JavaScript code
    81→2. Use `Uint8Array` for binary data, not strings
    82→3. Use Web Crypto API for browser encryption
    83→4. Use camelCase for API fields, snake_case for database columns
    84→5. Include proper error handling for crypto operations
    85→6. Clear sensitive data from memory after use
    86→
    87→## Architecture Decisions
    88→
    89→- **Auth:** Web3Auth for key derivation, CipherBox backend for tokens
    90→- **Storage:** IPFS via Kubo for files, IPNS for metadata (all relayed via CipherBox API)
    91→- **Encryption:** Client-side only, server is zero-knowledge
    92→- **Sync:** IPNS polling (30s interval), no push infrastructure
    93→- **Desktop:** FUSE mount for transparent file access
    94→- **TEE Republishing:** Phala Cloud (primary) / AWS Nitro (fallback) for automatic IPNS republishing every 3 hours
    95→- **Key Epochs:** TEE public keys rotate with 4-week grace period for seamless migration
    96→
    97→## Out of Scope (v1.0)
    98→
    99→Do not implement or suggest implementations for:
   100→
   101→- Billing/payments
   102→- File versioning
   103→- File/folder sharing
   104→- Mobile apps
   105→- Search/indexing
   106→- Collaborative editing
   107→- Team accounts
   108→
   109→## Verification with MCP Tools
   110→
   111→### Playwright MCP Verification (REQUIRED)
   112→
   113→**ALWAYS attempt to verify application changes using Playwright MCP** when it is available. This ensures implemented features work correctly at runtime.
   114→
   115→**When to use Playwright MCP:**
   116→
   117→- After implementing UI components
   118→- After modifying styles or layouts
   119→- After adding new pages or routes
   120→- After any user-facing changes
   121→- During GSD verification phase
   122→
   123→**Verification workflow:**
   124→
   125→```typescript
   126→// 1. Navigate to the app
   127→await mcp__playwright__navigate({ url: 'http://localhost:5173' });
   128→
   129→// 2. Wait for page to load
   130→await mcp__playwright__wait({ selector: '[data-testid="app-loaded"]' });
   131→
   132→// 3. Capture screenshot for visual verification
   133→await mcp__playwright__screenshot({ fullPage: true, name: 'verification' });
   134→
   135→// 4. Verify element existence
   136→const exists = await mcp__playwright__evaluate({
   137→  script: `!!document.querySelector('.expected-element')`,
   138→});
   139→
   140→// 5. Verify computed styles (for UI work)
   141→const styles = await mcp__playwright__evaluate({
   142→  script: `
   143→    const el = document.querySelector('.target');
   144→    const s = getComputedStyle(el);
   145→    return { backgroundColor: s.backgroundColor, color: s.color };
   146→  `,
   147→});
   148→
   149→// 6. Test interactions
   150→await mcp__playwright__click({ selector: 'button.action' });
   151→await mcp__playwright__wait({ selector: '.result-element' });
   152→```
   153→
   154→**If Playwright MCP is not available:**
   155→
   156→- Document what needs human verification
   157→- Provide manual test steps
   158→- Flag items in VERIFICATION.md
   159→
   160→### Pencil MCP for Design Work
   161→
   162→**When working on UI phases**, use Pencil MCP (if available) or parse `.pen` files directly to extract design specifications as the source of truth.
   163→
   164→**Design files location:** `designs/*.pen`
   165→
   166→**Verification against design:**
   167→
   168→1. Extract design specs from Pencil file
   169→2. Verify CSS values match exactly (hex codes, pixel values)
   170→3. Use Playwright MCP to verify computed styles at runtime
   171→4. Document any discrepancies with file/line references
   172→
   173→**Reference:** See `.claude/get-shit-done/references/pencil-design-workflow.md`
   174→
   175→## Git Workflow
   176→
   177→**Branch Protection Rules:**
   178→
   179→- **NEVER push directly to `main` branch** - all changes must go through feature branches and PRs
   180→- Create feature branches with descriptive names (e.g., `feat/add-auth`, `fix/ipns-publish`)
   181→- All commits should be made on feature branches first
   182→- Merge to main only via pull requests
   183→
   184→**Branch Naming:**
   185→
   186→- `feat/` - New features
   187→- `fix/` - Bug fixes
   188→- `docs/` - Documentation updates
   189→- `refactor/` - Code refactoring
   190→- `chore/` - Maintenance tasks
   191→
   192→**Commit Messages:**
   193→
   194→Commits must follow the [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) format. This is enforced by commitlint via a husky `commit-msg` hook.
   195→
   196→```text
   197→type(optional-scope): description
   198→
   199→[optional body]
   200→```
   201→
   202→Valid types: `feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `style`, `refactor`, `perf`, `test`, `build`, `ci`, `chore`, `revert`. Any scope string is allowed (e.g., `feat(api): add health endpoint`).
   203→
   204→**Releases & Versioning:**
   205→
   206→- All packages share a single unified version (currently tracked in `.release-please-manifest.json`)
   207→- [Release Please](https://github.com/googleapis/release-please) automates changelog generation, version bumping, and GitHub Releases
   208→- On push to `main`, the `release-please.yml` workflow creates/updates a release PR with accumulated changes
   209→- When that PR is merged, Release Please creates a GitHub Release and `vX.Y.Z` tag
   210→- Version bumps propagate to all `package.json` files, `Cargo.toml`, and `tauri.conf.json` via `release-please-config.json`
   211→- Staging deploys are triggered by pushing a tag matching `v*-staging*`. Legacy tags (e.g., `v0.1.0-staging.1`) also match but the **recommended convention** for new tags is `v<version>-staging-rc-<N>` (e.g., `v0.1.2-staging-rc-1`, `v0.1.2-staging-rc-2`). The `rc-N` suffix is a sequential counter allowing multiple staging deploys per version.
   212→

{
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.github/copilot-instructions.md",
  "content": "# CipherBox AI Agent Instructions\n\n## Project Overview\n\n**CipherBox** is a **technology demonstrator** for privacy-first, zero-knowledge encrypted cloud storage using IPFS/IPNS for decentralized persistence and Web3Auth for deterministic key derivation.\n\n**Purpose:** This is not a commercial product. It demonstrates novel applications of cryptography and decentralized systems.\n\n**Core Principle:** The server NEVER sees plaintext data or unencrypted keys. All encryption/decryption happens client-side.\n\n## Documentation\n\n| Document                                                                   | Purpose                                        |\n| :------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------- |\n| [README.md](../README.md)                                                  | Project overview, features, tech stack          |\n| [docs/ARCHITECTURE.md](../docs/ARCHITECTURE.md)                            | Encryption hierarchy, key derivation, data flows, threat model |\n| [docs/DEVELOPMENT.md](../docs/DEVELOPMENT.md)                              | Local setup, running, testing                  |\n| [docs/AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md](../docs/AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md) | Auth flow details                           |\n| [docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md](../docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md)                    | All metadata object schemas with field tables  |\n| [docs/VAULT_EXPORT_FORMAT.md](../docs/VAULT_EXPORT_FORMAT.md)              | Export/recovery data format                    |\n| [docs/DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md](../docs/DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md) | Migration discipline                         |\n\n---\n\n## Critical Architecture Patterns\n\n### Two-Phase Authentication Model\n\nCipherBox uses a **mandatory two-phase auth flow**:\n\n1. **Phase 1 (Web3Auth MPC Core Kit):** User authenticates via email OTP, Google OAuth, magic link, or external wallet. Web3Auth derives an ECDSA secp256k1 keypair via MPC threshold cryptography. MFA is handled via device factors.\n2. **Phase 2 (CipherBox Backend):** Client authenticates with the backend using a Web3Auth ID token (JWT) validated via JWKS. The backend issues its own access/refresh token pair.\n\n**Key Insight:** The ECDSA keypair from Phase 1 is the user's identity. Web3Auth ensures the same user always derives the same keypair regardless of auth method (when methods resolve to the same CipherBox `userId`).\n\nSee [docs/AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md](../docs/AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md) for full details.\n\n### Zero-Knowledge Key Hierarchy\n\n```text\nUser Auth → Web3Auth MPC Core Kit → ECDSA Private Key (client RAM only, never transmitted)\n                    ├─ Used for ECIES decryption of all data keys\n                    ├─ Used to derive IPNS signing keys (Ed25519, HKDF)\n                    └─ Destroyed on logout\n\nECDSA Public Key (stored on server, identifies user)\n    └─ Used to encrypt (ECIES):\n        ├─ Root Folder Key (random AES-256, stored encrypted on server)\n        ├─ Subfolder Keys (random AES-256, stored encrypted in parent folder metadata)\n        └─ File Keys (random AES-256, stored encrypted in per-file metadata)\n\nIPNS Keypairs (Ed25519, HKDF-derived from private key)\n    ├─ Vault IPNS (root folder metadata pointer)\n    ├─ Device Registry IPNS\n    └─ Per-file IPNS (file metadata pointer)\n```\n\n**Critical Rule:** Never log, persist to disk, or transmit the ECDSA private key. It exists ONLY in client memory during the session.\n\n### v2 Folder Metadata with FilePointers\n\nEach folder has its own IPNS entry. Files use a v2 `FilePointer` schema — the folder stores a slim reference, and per-file crypto material lives in a dedicated IPNS record.\n\nSee [docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md](../docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md) for the full schema reference including `FolderMetadata`, `FilePointer`, `FileMetadata`, and all other metadata objects.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\n| Component          | Technology                                    |\n| :----------------- | :-------------------------------------------- |\n| **Frontend**       | React 18 + TypeScript + Tailwind CSS          |\n| **Backend**        | Node.js + NestJS + TypeScript                 |\n| **Database**       | PostgreSQL 16                                 |\n| **Job Queue**      | BullMQ + Redis                                |\n| **Key Derivation** | Web3Auth MPC Core Kit (`@web3auth/mpc-core-kit`) |\n| **Storage**        | IPFS via Kubo                                 |\n| **Desktop**        | Tauri v2 + FUSE-T (macOS, SMB backend)        |\n| **TEE**            | Phala Cloud (IPNS republishing)               |\n| **Crypto**         | Web Crypto API (AES-256-GCM, ECIES secp256k1) |\n\n### Encryption Primitives (Non-Negotiable)\n\n| Algorithm           | Use Case                             | Implementation    |\n| ------------------- | ------------------------------------ | ----------------- |\n| **AES-256-GCM**     | File content + metadata encryption   | Web Crypto API    |\n| **AES-256-CTR**     | Streaming encryption (large files)   | Web Crypto API    |\n| **ECIES secp256k1** | Key wrapping (files, folders, IPNS)  | eciesjs           |\n| **HKDF-SHA256**     | IPNS keypair derivation              | Web Crypto API    |\n| **Ed25519**         | IPNS record signing                  | @noble/ed25519    |\n\n**Never use:** Custom crypto, CBC mode, ECB mode, MD5, SHA1\n\n## API Endpoints (Actual Implementation)\n\n| Endpoint                        | Method | Purpose                              |\n| :------------------------------ | :----- | :----------------------------------- |\n| `/auth/identity/google`         | POST   | Google OAuth authentication          |\n| `/auth/identity/email/send-otp` | POST   | Send email OTP                       |\n| `/auth/identity/email/verify-otp` | POST | Verify email OTP                     |\n| `/auth/identity/wallet/nonce`   | GET    | Get nonce for wallet auth            |\n| `/auth/identity/wallet`         | POST   | Wallet signature authentication      |\n| `/vault`                        | GET    | Get vault data                       |\n| `/vault/init`                   | POST   | Initialize vault (first-time setup)  |\n| `/vault/config`                 | GET    | Get vault configuration              |\n| `/vault/export`                 | GET    | Generate vault export                |\n| `/vault/quota`                  | GET    | Get storage quota                    |\n| `/ipfs/upload`                  | POST   | Upload encrypted file (multipart/form-data) |\n| `/ipfs/unpin`                   | POST   | Unpin CID from IPFS                  |\n| `/ipfs/:cid`                    | GET    | Fetch file by CID                    |\n| `/ipns/publish`                 | POST   | Publish IPNS record                  |\n| `/ipns/publish-batch`           | POST   | Batch publish IPNS records           |\n| `/ipns/resolve`                 | GET    | Resolve IPNS name to CID            |\n| `/device-approval/request`      | POST   | Request device approval              |\n| `/device-approval/pending`      | GET    | Get pending approvals                |\n| `/shares`                       | POST   | Create a share                       |\n| `/shares/received`              | GET    | Get received shares                  |\n\n## Development Patterns\n\n### API Development Workflow\n\nWhen working on `apps/api` code:\n\n1. **After modifying API endpoints, DTOs, or controllers**, regenerate the API client:\n\n   ```bash\n   pnpm api:generate\n   ```\n\n2. **Always run `pnpm api:generate` before completing a feature** that touches the API.\n\n3. **Commit the regenerated client files** (`apps/web/src/api/`) along with your API changes.\n\n### File Upload Flow (Reference)\n\n```typescript\n// 1. Generate random file key\nconst fileKey = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(32));\nconst fileIV = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(12));\n\n// 2. Encrypt file content\nconst encryptedFile = await crypto.subtle.encrypt(\n  { name: 'AES-GCM', iv: fileIV },\n  await crypto.subtle.importKey('raw', fileKey, 'AES-GCM', false, ['encrypt']),\n  fileContent\n);\n\n// 3. Wrap file key with user's public key (ECIES)\nconst encryptedFileKey = await eciesEncrypt(fileKey, userPublicKey);\n\n// 4. Upload encrypted file to backend → IPFS → get CID\n// POST /ipfs/upload (multipart/form-data with 'file' field)\nconst formData = new FormData();\nformData.append('file', new Blob([encryptedFile]));\nconst { cid } = await api.post('/ipfs/upload', formData);\n// NOTE: No fileName sent — the server never sees plaintext file names\n\n// 5. Create per-file FileMetadata (own IPNS record)\n// Contains: cid, fileKeyEncrypted, fileIv, size, etc.\n\n// 6. Add FilePointer to folder metadata children\n// Contains: type, nameEncrypted, nameIv, fileMetaIpnsName\n\n// 7. Re-encrypt folder metadata and republish IPNS\nawait republishFolderIPNS(folderId, updatedMetadata);\n```\n\n### IPNS Publishing Pattern\n\nEvery write operation (create/rename/move/delete) must:\n\n1. Update the in-memory folder metadata\n2. Re-encrypt the entire metadata JSON with the folder key\n3. Upload encrypted metadata to IPFS → get new CID\n4. Publish IPNS record: `/ipns/k51...` → `/ipfs/<new CID>`\n\n**Performance Note:** IPNS publishing is the bottleneck (~2s). Batch operations when possible.\n\n## Common Pitfalls & Anti-Patterns\n\n### Never Do This\n\n1. **Store keys in plaintext localStorage** — use in-memory state only\n2. **Log sensitive data** — no `console.log` of keys, decrypted content\n3. **Send plaintext to server** — all file content and names must be encrypted client-side\n4. **Send file names to server** — the server is zero-knowledge, it never sees plaintext names\n5. **Use sync crypto in main thread** — use Web Workers for large files\n\n### Correct Patterns\n\n1. **Store keys in memory, clear on logout** (React context / Zustand)\n2. **Use Web Workers for large file encryption**\n3. **Use `Uint8Array` for all binary data**, not strings\n4. **Use `camelCase` for API fields, `snake_case` for database columns**\n\n## MVP Scope Boundaries (v1.0)\n\n### In Scope\n\n- Multi-method auth (email OTP, Google OAuth, magic link, external wallet)\n- File upload/download (E2E encrypted)\n- Folder operations (create/rename/move/delete)\n- Web UI (React) + Desktop mount (macOS FUSE via Tauri v2)\n- Multi-device sync (IPNS polling, ~30s latency)\n- Vault export (data portability)\n- TEE-based IPNS republishing (Phala Cloud)\n\n### Out of Scope (defer to v1.1+)\n\n- Billing/payments\n- File versioning\n- Search/indexing\n- Mobile apps\n- Linux/Windows desktop\n\n## Testing\n\n### Security Test Checklist\n\n- [ ] Private key never logged (search logs for \"privateKey\", \"ecdsa\", \"0x04\")\n- [ ] Private key never in localStorage/sessionStorage\n- [ ] All file uploads: server receives ciphertext only\n- [ ] All folder/file names: encrypted in metadata, never sent plaintext\n- [ ] IPNS signature verification passes\n- [ ] Wrong private key cannot decrypt vault\n\n### Running Tests\n\n```bash\npnpm test              # Unit tests (all workspaces)\npnpm test:e2e          # Playwright E2E (Playwright starts API/web automatically)\npnpm typecheck         # TypeScript type checking\n```\n\n## Verification with MCP Tools\n\n### Playwright MCP for Application Testing\n\n**ALWAYS attempt to verify application changes using Playwright MCP** when available.\n\n**If Playwright MCP is not available:**\n\n- Document what needs manual verification\n- Provide step-by-step test instructions\n\n### Pencil MCP for Design Work\n\nFor UI work, verify implementations against Pencil design files at `designs/*.pen`.\n\n## Questions to Ask When Unclear\n\n1. **Auth-related:** \"Does this require Phase 1 (Web3Auth) or Phase 2 (CipherBox backend) authentication?\"\n2. **Encryption:** \"Should this data be encrypted client-side before transmission?\"\n3. **Keys:** \"Which key in the hierarchy (ECDSA private, root folder, subfolder, file) should be used here?\"\n4. **IPNS:** \"Does this operation require republishing an IPNS entry?\"\n5. **Scope:** \"Is this feature in v1.0 MVP scope, or should it be deferred to v1.1+?\"\n\n## Final Note\n\nThis project prioritizes **cryptographic correctness over convenience**. When in doubt, err on the side of more encryption, more validation, and stricter security.\n\n**For detailed guidance:** See [docs/ARCHITECTURE.md](../docs/ARCHITECTURE.md) for crypto design and [docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md](../docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md) for all metadata object schemas.\n"
}
```

     1→# Codebase Concerns
     2→
     3→**Analysis Date:** 2026-03-06
     4→
     5→## Tech Debt
     6→
     7→**Orphaned IPNS records on file/folder deletion:**
     8→- Issue: When files or folders are deleted, their IPNS records and TEE republish enrollments are not cleaned up. The code logs warnings and defers to "Phase 14" via TODO comments.
     9→- Files: `apps/web/src/services/folder.service.ts:455`, `apps/web/src/services/folder.service.ts:507-512`, `apps/web/src/services/delete.service.ts:22-29`
    10→- Impact: Orphaned IPNS records accumulate in the TEE republish schedule. The republish service has capacity warnings at 1000+ records. Each orphan wastes TEE compute and delegated routing bandwidth every 3 hours until natural expiry.
    11→- Fix approach: The API already has `unenrollIpns()` at `apps/api/src/republish/republish.service.ts:255`. Expose it via a REST endpoint and call it from the web client during file/folder deletion. Batch unenrollment needed for folder deletes containing multiple files.
    12→
    13→**Desktop device approval polling not implemented:**
    14→- Issue: Phase 11.2 TODO comments indicate the desktop app lacks approval notification polling. When another device needs MFA approval, the desktop user has no notification.
    15→- Files: `apps/desktop/src/main.ts:32`, `apps/desktop/src/auth.ts:680`
    16→- Impact: Desktop users must use the web app to approve new devices. Reduces desktop app self-sufficiency.
    17→- Fix approach: Add a background polling interval (similar to web's `useDeviceApproval`) that checks for pending approvals and surfaces native OS notifications via Tauri's notification API.
    18→
    19→**Legacy POC directory still in repo:**
    20→- Issue: `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/` (702-line monolithic file) remains alongside production code. Uses deprecated `ipfs-http-client@60.0.1`, has no tests, and uses patterns explicitly superseded by the production implementation.
    21→- Files: `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/src/index.ts`, `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/package.json`
    22→- Impact: New contributors may confuse PoC with current implementation. Adds noise to searches and dependency audits.
    23→- Fix approach: Already marked as historical reference per CLAUDE.md. Consider moving to a separate branch or archive tag if repo size is a concern.
    24→
    25→**Large monolithic files in web app:**
    26→- Issue: Several web app files exceed 900 lines, concentrating multiple responsibilities.
    27→- Files: `apps/web/src/services/folder.service.ts` (1080 lines), `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/FileBrowser.tsx` (964 lines), `apps/web/src/services/bin.service.ts` (962 lines), `apps/web/src/hooks/useFolderMutations.ts` (793 lines), `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts` (522 lines), `apps/web/src/hooks/useSharedNavigation.ts` (551 lines)
    28→- Impact: Difficult to test individual behaviors in isolation. Cognitive load for code review. Higher risk of merge conflicts.
    29→- Fix approach: Extract folder CRUD operations from `folder.service.ts` into focused modules (create, delete, update, navigate). Split `FileBrowser.tsx` into container and presentational components. Extract bin operations into smaller service files.
    30→
    31→**Pervasive console.log/warn/error in web app:**
    32→- Issue: 50+ `console.error`/`console.warn`/`console.log` calls throughout production web code instead of a structured logging abstraction.
    33→- Files: `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/FileBrowser.tsx` (12 calls), `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts` (10 calls), `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/BinBrowser.tsx` (5 calls), `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/ShareDialog.tsx` (3 calls), and many more across hooks and services.
    34→- Impact: No log level filtering, no structured output, no ability to ship logs to an observability service. Debug logs leak into production.
    35→- Fix approach: Introduce a lightweight logging wrapper (e.g., `lib/logger.ts`) that wraps `console.*` with level filtering and optional structured output. Replace all direct `console.*` calls.
    36→
    37→**Silenced unpin failures across the codebase:**
    38→- Issue: All IPFS unpin calls use `.catch(() => {})` pattern, silently swallowing failures.
    39→- Files: `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/ReplaceFileDialog.tsx:53,64,73`, `apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts:156`, `apps/web/src/hooks/useFileVersions.ts:142,261`, `apps/web/src/hooks/useFileOperations.ts:463`, `apps/web/src/services/bin.service.ts:717,748,826,842`
    40→- Impact: Failed unpins mean orphaned data on IPFS that consumes storage quota without the user knowing. Over time this could exhaust the user's 500 MiB quota with unreachable data.
    41→- Fix approach: Log unpin failures (at minimum) and consider a periodic background reconciliation that retries failed unpins. Track unpin failures in a local queue.
    42→
    43→**`any` type usage in web app:**
    44→- Issue: Several `as any` casts remain, primarily around Web3Auth SDK integration and polyfills.
    45→- Files: `apps/web/src/main.tsx:6,64`, `apps/web/src/lib/web3auth/hooks.ts:146,152`, `apps/web/src/polyfills.ts:6-9`, `apps/web/src/stores/folder.store.ts:185`
    46→- Impact: Type safety gaps around authentication flow. Web3Auth SDK types are poorly defined, making it hard to eliminate.
    47→- Fix approach: Create typed wrappers for Web3Auth SDK interactions. The polyfill `any` casts are acceptable (Node.js globals on `window`).
    48→
    49→## Known Bugs
    50→
    51→**No active known bugs identified in the current codebase.**
    52→
    53→Previous known bugs (upload modal stuck, auth refresh race) were fixed in PRs #56 and #58. The IPNS resolve 502 issue (delegated-ipfs.dev unreliability) is mitigated by DB-cached CID fallback and retry logic in `apps/api/src/ipns/delegated-routing.client.ts`.
    54→
    55→## Security Considerations
    56→
    57→**Memory zeroing is best-effort in JavaScript:**
    58→- Risk: `clearBytes()` / `.fill(0)` cannot guarantee sensitive key material is erased from V8 heap, JIT-compiled code, or GC intermediaries.
    59→- Files: `packages/crypto/src/utils/memory.ts`, `apps/web/src/stores/vault.store.ts:73-81`, `apps/web/src/stores/auth.store.ts:74-77`, `apps/web/src/stores/folder.store.ts:137-169`
    60→- Current mitigation: The codebase consistently uses `.fill(0)` on key buffers during logout and store cleanup. The crypto package documents the limitation clearly.
    61→- Recommendations: This is an inherent JavaScript limitation. For the desktop app, the Rust side should use `zeroize` crate for key material. Current approach is acceptable for browser context.
    62→
    63→**Web3Auth localStorage usage:**
    64→- Risk: Web3Auth MPC Core Kit stores its share factor in `localStorage`, which is accessible to XSS.
    65→- Files: `apps/web/src/lib/web3auth/core-kit.ts:24` (`storage: window.localStorage`)
    66→- Current mitigation: CipherBox's own keys (vault keypair, folder keys) are never stored in localStorage -- only in Zustand memory stores. The Web3Auth factor in localStorage is one share of a 2-of-3 TSS scheme (insufficient alone to derive the private key).
    67→- Recommendations: CSP headers and XSS prevention remain critical. The MFA enrollment (Phase 12) adds a device approval factor that further limits exposure.
    68→
    69→**IPFS node credentials and access control:**
    70→- Risk: Kubo API endpoint has no built-in authentication. Anyone with network access to port 5001 can pin/unpin content.
    71→- Files: `apps/api/src/ipfs/providers/local.provider.ts:14-24`, `apps/api/.env.example`
    72→- Current mitigation: Kubo API bound to localhost in dev. In staging/production, Docker network isolation limits access to the API container.
    73→- Recommendations: For production, use reverse proxy with auth or Kubo's API access controls. Consider network-level firewall rules.
    74→
    75→**Test login endpoint available in staging:**
    76→- Risk: `POST /auth/test-login` bypasses all real authentication. Available when `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` is set and `NODE_ENV !== 'production'`.
    77→- Files: `apps/api/.env.example:49-51`, `apps/api/src/auth/` (test-auth service)
    78→- Current mitigation: Guarded by `NODE_ENV` check and requires knowing the secret. Not available in production.
    79→- Recommendations: Ensure CI/CD pipeline never sets `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` in production environment. Add monitoring alert if the endpoint is called in staging.
    80→
    81→**CORS wildcard patterns:**
    82→- Risk: CORS configuration supports wildcard patterns (e.g., `https://cipher-box-pr-*.onrender.com`) which could be abused if an attacker can create matching subdomains on the hosting platform.
    83→- Files: `apps/api/src/main.ts:29-47`
    84→- Current mitigation: Wildcards only match specific hosting platform patterns.
    85→- Recommendations: Use exact origin lists in production. Reserve wildcards for PR preview environments only.
    86→
    87→## Performance Bottlenecks
    88→
    89→**FUSE FilePointer resolution blocks filesystem thread:**
    90→- Problem: After background metadata refresh, unresolved FilePointers are resolved synchronously on the single FUSE thread with `O(N * timeout)` latency.
    91→- Files: `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs:1039-1049`
    92→- Cause: Each FilePointer resolution requires an IPNS resolve network call. The TODO at line 1039 documents this.
    93→- Improvement path: Spawn async tasks via a channel pair (like the existing `refresh_tx/rx` pattern) to avoid stalling the FUSE thread on network I/O.
    94→
    95→**Full file content buffering for AES-GCM encryption:**
    96→- Problem: Files encrypted with GCM mode are fully loaded into memory. The 100 MB file size limit means up to 100 MB of memory per concurrent upload.
    97→- Files: `apps/web/src/services/upload.service.ts`, `packages/crypto/src/aes/encrypt.ts`
    98→- Cause: AES-256-GCM requires full content for authentication tag computation.
    99→- Improvement path: AES-256-CTR streaming encryption already exists (`packages/crypto/src/aes/encrypt-ctr.ts`, `packages/crypto/src/aes/decrypt-ctr.ts`) and is used for media streaming playback. Extend CTR usage to all uploads for reduced memory pressure. Desktop already uses CTR for FUSE reads.
   100→
   101→**IPNS polling for sync (30-second interval):**
   102→- Problem: Sync latency is at least 30 seconds. No push notification infrastructure exists.
   103→- Files: `apps/web/src/hooks/useSyncPolling.ts`, per `TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md` Section 5.4
   104→- Cause: IPNS is pull-based. Adding WebSocket push would require backend infrastructure.
   105→- Improvement path: Future versions could implement WebSocket notifications for immediate sync triggers, falling back to polling.
   106→
   107→**No pagination for large folders:**
   108→- Problem: Folder metadata contains all children inline. A folder with 1000 files loads all 1000 entries into memory and renders them all at once.
   109→- Files: `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/FileList.tsx`, `apps/web/src/services/folder.service.ts`
   110→- Cause: IPNS-based metadata is a single encrypted blob per folder.
   111→- Improvement path: Implement virtual scrolling in the UI (render only visible rows). The 1000-file limit per PRD mitigates the data loading issue.
   112→
   113→## Fragile Areas
   114→
   115→**FUSE-T SMB backend on macOS:**
   116→- Files: `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs` (1803 lines), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/write_ops.rs` (976 lines), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/vendor/fuser/src/channel.rs`
   117→- Why fragile: FUSE-T is a userspace NFS/SMB translation layer, not kernel FUSE. Numerous workarounds exist for macOS-specific issues (SMB opendir requires non-zero fh, rename truncates filenames by 8 bytes, UID mismatch under SMB proxy, no FSEvents). Each macOS update could introduce new kernel-side behavior changes.
   118→- Safe modification: Always test with Finder, Terminal `ls`/`mv`/`cp`, and multi-file operations. Single-thread constraint means any blocking call stalls everything.
   119→- Test coverage: Manual testing only. No automated FUSE integration tests. The `tests.rs` files cover crypto operations but not filesystem operations.
   120→
   121→**Windows FUSE implementation (WinFSP):**
   122→- Files: `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/windows/mod.rs` (498 lines), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/windows/operations.rs` (694 lines), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/windows/write_ops.rs` (997 lines), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/windows/read_ops.rs` (430 lines)
   123→- Why fragile: 2824 lines of platform-specific FUSE code with many `lock().unwrap()` calls that will panic on poisoned mutex. No automated tests. Uses WinFSP which has different semantics from macOS FUSE-T.
   124→- Safe modification: Test on actual Windows with Explorer, cmd, and PowerShell. Watch for mutex poisoning under error conditions.
   125→- Test coverage: No automated tests exist for Windows FUSE operations.
   126→
   127→**Vendored fuser crate:**
   128→- Files: `apps/desktop/src-tauri/vendor/fuser/` (~5000 lines), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/vendor/fuser/src/channel.rs` (critical patch)
   129→- Why fragile: Vendored fork of fuser 0.16 with socket-read patch for FUSE-T compatibility. Upstream updates cannot be trivially merged. The patch is load-bearing -- without it, large file writes crash the FUSE session.
   130→- Safe modification: Never update without re-applying the `channel.rs` receive() patch. Document patch diff in vendor directory.
   131→- Test coverage: No tests for the patched receive() function.
   132→
   133→**Delegated routing (delegated-ipfs.dev) dependency:**
   134→- Files: `apps/api/src/ipns/delegated-routing.client.ts`
   135→- Why fragile: The external service at `delegated-ipfs.dev` has been unreliable historically (502 errors documented in memory). It is the sole path for IPNS record publishing and resolution from the API.
   136→- Safe modification: The client has retry with exponential backoff (3 retries, 1s base delay, 30s cap). Changes to the API or rate limits could break publishing.
   137→- Test coverage: Unit tests at `apps/api/src/ipns/delegated-routing.client.spec.ts` cover retry logic. No integration tests against real service.
   138→
   139→**Web3Auth MPC Core Kit integration:**
   140→- Files: `apps/web/src/lib/web3auth/core-kit.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/web3auth/hooks.ts` (multiple `as any` casts), `apps/web/src/hooks/useAuth.ts` (522 lines)
   141→- Why fragile: Web3Auth SDK has poor TypeScript definitions (`any` casts required). The `REQUIRED_SHARE` state handling at `hooks.ts:205-226` works around a bug where Web3Auth doesn't auto-check localStorage for device factors. SDK version upgrades frequently change behavior.
   142→- Safe modification: Test all auth flows (email, Google, wallet) end-to-end after any Web3Auth dependency update.
   143→- Test coverage: Auth flow tested via E2E (`tests/e2e/tests/full-workflow.spec.ts`) but Web3Auth unit mocking is complex.
   144→
   145→## Scaling Limits
   146→
   147→**IPNS record propagation and TEE republishing:**
   148→- Current capacity: TEE republishes all enrolled IPNS records every 3 hours via batch endpoint.
   149→- Limit: At 1000+ enrolled records per user, republish cycles may exceed the 3-hour window. The `delete.service.ts:28` documents this threshold.
   150→- Scaling path: Implement IPNS unenrollment on deletion (see Tech Debt section). Consider per-user republish prioritization.
   151→
   152→**Folder metadata size (1000 files per folder):**
   153→- Current capacity: PRD constrains to 1000 children per folder.
   154→- Limit: Metadata blob grows linearly with children count. With FilePointers (~100 bytes each), a 1000-file folder produces ~100 KB of metadata before encryption.
   155→- Scaling path: This limit is enforced by design. For larger collections, users must create subfolders.
   156→
   157→**File size limit (100 MB with GCM):**
   158→- Current capacity: 100 MB per file per PRD constraint.
   159→- Limit: Browser memory pressure with full-file buffering for GCM encryption.
   160→- Scaling path: CTR streaming encryption is implemented but not yet the default for uploads. Switching to CTR for large files would remove the memory constraint.
   161→
   162→**Single Kubo IPFS node:**
   163→- Current capacity: One Kubo node handles all pinning/unpinning for the entire deployment.
   164→- Limit: Single point of failure. Kubo node downtime = no uploads or downloads.
   165→- Scaling path: Add IPFS cluster for redundancy, or migrate to a managed IPFS pinning service (Pinata, web3.storage).
   166→
   167→## Dependencies at Risk
   168→
   169→**delegated-ipfs.dev (external service):**
   170→- Risk: Third-party service with documented unreliability. No SLA. Single point of failure for IPNS operations.
   171→- Impact: If down, no IPNS records can be published or resolved. File metadata becomes temporarily inaccessible.
   172→- Migration plan: DB-cached CID fallback exists for resolution. Consider self-hosting a delegated routing endpoint or adding a secondary provider.
   173→
   174→**Web3Auth MPC Core Kit (@web3auth/mpc-core-kit@^3.5.0):**
   175→- Risk: Complex SDK with frequent breaking changes. Poor TypeScript types require `any` casts. Authentication is entirely dependent on Web3Auth infrastructure.
   176→- Impact: SDK updates may break auth flows. Web3Auth service downtime = no new logins (existing sessions continue via refresh tokens).
   177→- Migration plan: The auth architecture separates Web3Auth (key derivation) from CipherBox auth (JWT tokens). A migration to a different MPC provider would require replacing only the Web3Auth integration layer.
   178→
   179→**eciesjs@^0.4.16:**
   180→- Risk: Small package with limited maintenance activity. Used for ECIES key wrapping (core security function).
   181→- Impact: Security vulnerability in this package would compromise key wrapping.
   182→- Migration plan: The package wraps noble/secp256k1 internally. Could be replaced with direct ECIES implementation using noble primitives.
   183→
   184→**FUSE-T (macOS userspace filesystem):**
   185→- Risk: Third-party macOS filesystem driver. Not a standard macOS component. Requires user installation.
   186→- Impact: macOS updates can break FUSE-T. The NFS-to-SMB backend switch was forced by a macOS Sequoia kernel bug.
   187→- Migration plan: Monitor FUSE-T releases. Linux build uses kernel FUSE (more stable). Consider FileProvider API on macOS as long-term alternative.
   188→
   189→## Missing Critical Features
   190→
   191→**No offline support (web or desktop):**
   192→- Problem: No service worker for offline caching in web app. Desktop FUSE mount requires continuous API connectivity.
   193→- Blocks: Users cannot access files when offline. Desktop mount becomes unresponsive without network.
   194→- Files: No service worker files exist (only a streaming-media SW at `apps/web/src/main.tsx:39`). Desktop FUSE operations fail with EIO on network errors.
   195→
   196→**No file versioning:**
   197→- Problem: Overwriting a file replaces the previous version entirely. No version history.
   198→- Blocks: No undo for accidental overwrites. Out of scope for v1.0 per CLAUDE.md.
   199→- Files: `apps/web/src/hooks/useFileVersions.ts` handles IPNS-based file metadata updates but not version history.
   200→
   201→**No monitoring/observability (web app):**
   202→- Problem: Web app has no error tracking service (Sentry, etc.). Errors are logged to `console.error` and lost.
   203→- Blocks: Cannot detect or diagnose production issues affecting users. API has Prometheus metrics (`apps/api/src/metrics/`) but web has nothing.
   204→- Files: `apps/web/src/main.tsx:9-14` wraps `console.error` but only for in-memory display.
   205→
   206→## Test Coverage Gaps
   207→
   208→**Web app has minimal unit tests (4 test files for 269 source files):**
   209→- What's not tested: All React components, most hooks, all services except sync store.
   210→- Files: Only 4 test files exist: `apps/web/src/stores/__tests__/sync-store.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/stores/__tests__/upload-error-recovery.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/stores/__tests__/logout-security.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/api/__tests__/client-refresh.test.ts`
   211→- Risk: Regressions in folder operations, file uploads, auth flows, sharing, and bin operations go undetected until E2E tests or manual testing. The 964-line `FileBrowser.tsx` has zero test coverage.
   212→- Priority: High. Focus first on services (`folder.service.ts`, `bin.service.ts`, `share.service.ts`) and critical hooks (`useAuth.ts`, `useFolderMutations.ts`).
   213→
   214→**TEE worker has zero tests:**
   215→- What's not tested: IPNS signing, key management, epoch rotation, auth middleware.
   216→- Files: `tee-worker/src/` (522 lines total, 0 test files)
   217→- Risk: Security-critical code (TEE key derivation, IPNS record signing) is untested. Regressions in epoch rotation or key decryption would silently break republishing.
   218→- Priority: High. The TEE worker handles decrypted IPNS private keys -- correctness is security-critical.
   219→
   220→**Desktop FUSE operations have no automated tests:**
   221→- What's not tested: All filesystem operations (read, write, create, rename, delete, mkdir), inode management, metadata caching, publish coordination.
   222→- Files: `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs` (1803 lines), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/write_ops.rs` (976 lines), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/inode.rs` (937 lines). Only `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/crypto/tests.rs` (1717 lines) covers the crypto layer.
   223→- Risk: FUSE bugs cause data loss or mount crashes. The single-thread constraint makes race conditions subtle. Manual testing is the only verification.
   224→- Priority: High. At minimum, add unit tests for `InodeTable` operations and `PublishCoordinator` logic.
   225→
   226→**Windows FUSE operations untested:**
   227→- What's not tested: All 2824 lines of Windows-specific FUSE code using WinFSP.
   228→- Files: `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/windows/` (5 files)
   229→- Risk: Platform-specific bugs (mutex poisoning from `lock().unwrap()`, different filesystem semantics) undetected.
   230→- Priority: Medium. Windows support appears to be secondary to macOS.
   231→
   232→**E2E test helpers are stubs:**
   233→- What's not tested: API helper functions for test setup are placeholder implementations.
   234→- Files: `tests/e2e/utils/api-helpers.ts:5-45` (3 TODO functions: `createTestUser`, `cleanupVault`, `seedTestFiles`)
   235→- Risk: E2E tests cannot programmatically set up complex test scenarios. Limited to UI-driven flows only.
   236→- Priority: Medium. Implement API helpers as more complex E2E scenarios are needed.
   237→
   238→---
   239→
   240→*Concerns audit: 2026-03-06*
   241→
```

4. Client sends encrypted blob to backend (POST `/ipfs/upload`, `multipart/form-data` field `file`)
```

Thread ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM5yekEC
Comment ID: PRRC_kwDOQ6DXvM6sdASS
Author: copilot-pull-request-reviewer
File: .planning/codebase/INTEGRATIONS.md:82
Body: This doc starts by listing IPFS/Web3Auth/Postgres/Redis/TEE as implemented integrations, but later sections still label core pieces as “Implemented in PoC” or “Planned” (e.g., IPFS/IPNS, Databases, Auth Provider). Please reconcile these sections so the document consistently reflects the current implementation vs. PoC-only pieces.

---
Thread ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM5yekEN
Comment ID: PRRC_kwDOQ6DXvM6sdASd
Author: copilot-pull-request-reviewer
File: .planning/codebase/STACK.md:139
Body: This file still contains several “planned/PoC” statements that don’t match the current implementation:
- “Planned Production” lists `@web3auth/modal`, but the repo uses `@web3auth/mpc-core-kit`.
- Ed25519 signing is described as “planned, via libsodium.js”, but the codebase uses `@noble/ed25519`.
- Dev platform requirements still say “npm or yarn”, but the repo is a pnpm workspace.
Please update these sections to avoid sending contributors to the wrong libraries/tooling.
```

     1→# Architecture
     2→
     3→**Analysis Date:** 2026-01-20
     4→
     5→## Pattern Overview
     6→
     7→**Overall:** Specification-First / Pre-Implementation Technology Demonstrator
     8→
     9→**Key Characteristics:**
    10→
    11→- Complete specification documents define the target architecture before implementation
    12→- Single proof-of-concept (PoC) console harness validates core IPFS/IPNS and encryption flows
    13→- Layered E2E encryption with zero-knowledge server design
    14→- Client-side cryptographic operations with server acting as relay and storage proxy
    15→- Per-folder IPNS records enabling future modular sharing capabilities
    16→
    17→## Layers
    18→
    19→**Specification Layer:**
    20→
    21→- Purpose: Define complete system design, security model, and implementation contract
    22→- Location: `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/`
    23→- Contains: PRD, technical architecture, API spec, data flows, client spec, implementation roadmap
    24→- Depends on: None (foundation documents)
    25→- Used by: All implementation code (when built)
    26→
    27→**Proof-of-Concept Layer:**
    28→
    29→- Purpose: Validate IPFS/IPNS assumptions and encryption flows end-to-end
    30→- Location: `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/`
    31→- Contains: Single-file console harness demonstrating file/folder operations
    32→- Depends on: IPFS daemon, cryptographic libraries
    33→- Used by: Architecture validation only (not production)
    34→
    35→**Client Layer (Specified, Not Implemented):**
    36→
    37→- Purpose: Handle all encryption/decryption, key management, UI
    38→- Location: (Not yet created - see `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/CLIENT_SPECIFICATION.md`)
    39→- Contains: React 18 web app, Tauri/Electron desktop app with FUSE
    40→- Depends on: Web3Auth SDK, Web Crypto API, CipherBox Backend API
    41→- Used by: End users
    42→
    43→**Backend Layer (Specified, Not Implemented):**
    44→
    45→- Purpose: Token management, IPFS/IPNS relay, vault metadata storage, TEE coordination
    46→- Location: (Not yet created - see `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/API_SPECIFICATION.md`)
    47→- Contains: NestJS API with 15 endpoints
    48→- Depends on: PostgreSQL, IPFS (Kubo), Web3Auth JWKS, TEE providers
    49→- Used by: Client applications
    50→
    51→**TEE Layer (Specified, Not Implemented):**
    52→
    53→- Purpose: Automatic IPNS republishing without user devices online
    54→- Location: External services (Phala Cloud primary, AWS Nitro fallback)
    55→- Contains: Secure enclaves for IPNS key decryption and signing
    56→- Depends on: Backend republish schedule
    57→- Used by: Backend cron jobs
    58→
    59→## Data Flow
    60→
    61→**File Upload Flow:**
    62→
    63→1. Client generates random `fileKey` (256-bit AES)
    64→2. Client encrypts file content with AES-256-GCM
    65→3. Client wraps `fileKey` with user's `publicKey` via ECIES
    66→4. Client sends encrypted blob to backend (POST `/ipfs/add`)
    67→5. Backend relays to IPFS (Kubo), returns CID
    68→6. Client updates folder metadata with new file entry
    69→7. Client encrypts metadata with `folderKey`
    70→8. Client signs IPNS record with folder's `ipnsPrivateKey`
    71→9. Client encrypts `ipnsPrivateKey` with TEE `publicKey` for republishing
    72→10. Client sends signed record + encrypted IPNS key to backend (POST `/ipns/publish`)
    73→11. Backend publishes to IPFS network and stores republish schedule
    74→
    75→**Authentication Flow:**
    76→
    77→1. User authenticates via Web3Auth (4 methods supported)
    78→2. Web3Auth derives deterministic ECDSA keypair (secp256k1)
    79→3. Client authenticates with CipherBox backend (JWT or SIWE signature)
    80→4. Backend verifies identity, issues access token (15min) + refresh token (7d)
    81→5. Backend returns encrypted vault keys and TEE public keys
    82→6. Client decrypts `rootFolderKey` using `privateKey`
    83→7. Session active until logout or token expiry
    84→
    85→**State Management:**
    86→
    87→- `privateKey`: Client RAM only, never persisted or transmitted
    88→- `rootFolderKey`: Server stores encrypted with ECIES, client decrypts on login
    89→- Folder/file keys: Stored encrypted in IPNS metadata records
    90→- `ipnsPrivateKey`: Client encrypts with TEE public key for republishing
    91→
    92→## Key Abstractions
    93→
    94→**Vault:**
    95→
    96→- Purpose: User's encrypted file storage namespace
    97→- Examples: Root folder IPNS record, associated metadata
    98→- Pattern: One vault per user, identified by `rootIpnsName`
    99→
   100→**Folder:**
   101→
   102→- Purpose: Encrypted container with child entries (files/folders)
   103→- Examples: Root folder, Documents, Archive (from PoC)
   104→- Pattern: Each folder has own IPNS keypair, `folderKey` for metadata encryption
   105→
   106→**File Entry:**
   107→
   108→- Purpose: Encrypted file reference within folder metadata
   109→- Examples: `hello.txt` in PoC
   110→- Pattern: Contains encrypted name, CID, wrapped `fileKey`, IV, encryption mode
   111→
   112→**TEE Key Epoch:**
   113→
   114→- Purpose: Rotation-safe TEE public key management
   115→- Examples: `currentEpoch`, `previousEpoch` for 4-week grace period
   116→- Pattern: Client encrypts IPNS keys with current epoch, TEE supports previous for migration
   117→
   118→## Entry Points
   119→
   120→**PoC Console Harness:**
   121→
   122→- Location: `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/src/index.ts`
   123→- Triggers: `npm run start` or `tsx src/index.ts`
   124→- Responsibilities: Creates folder hierarchy, uploads/modifies/moves/deletes files, verifies IPNS resolution, cleans up (unpin, remove keys)
   125→
   126→**Planned Web Application:**
   127→
   128→- Location: (To be created)
   129→- Triggers: Browser navigation to `/auth`, `/vault`, `/settings`
   130→- Responsibilities: Authentication, file browser UI, encryption/decryption
   131→
   132→**Planned Backend API:**
   133→
   134→- Location: (To be created)
   135→- Triggers: HTTP requests to 15 endpoints
   136→- Responsibilities: Token management, IPFS/IPNS relay, vault storage, TEE coordination
   137→
   138→**Planned Desktop Application:**
   139→
   140→- Location: (To be created)
   141→- Triggers: App launch, FUSE mount at `~/CipherVault`
   142→- Responsibilities: Transparent file access, background sync
   143→
   144→## Error Handling
   145→
   146→**Strategy:** Fail-fast with explicit error messages
   147→
   148→**Patterns:**
   149→
   150→- PoC: Throws errors on failures, logs warnings for non-critical issues (e.g., pin removal)
   151→- Specified Backend: HTTP status codes with structured error responses
   152→- Specified Client: User-facing error messages, retry logic for transient failures
   153→- Backend Republish Scheduler: Exponential backoff for failed entries (30s -> 60s -> 120s -> ... -> 3600s max)
   154→
   155→## Cross-Cutting Concerns
   156→
   157→**Logging:**
   158→
   159→- PoC: Console logging with step markers (`=== Step Name ===`)
   160→- Backend (specified): Structured logging, no private keys in logs (Security criterion S8)
   161→
   162→**Validation:**
   163→
   164→- PoC: Runtime type checking via TypeScript
   165→- Backend (specified): Request validation via NestJS decorators
   166→- Client (specified): Input sanitization before encryption
   167→
   168→**Authentication:**
   169→
   170→- Two-phase: Web3Auth for key derivation, CipherBox backend for API tokens
   171→- Token rotation: Refresh tokens rotated on each use
   172→- Zero-knowledge: Backend never sees private keys or plaintext
   173→
   174→**Security Invariants:**
   175→
   176→- Private key exists only in client RAM, cleared on logout
   177→- All files encrypted with unique random key + IV (no deduplication)
   178→- Server stores only encrypted keys (ECIES wrapped)
   179→- TEE keys exist in enclave memory only for milliseconds during signing
   180→
   181→---
   182→
   183→Architecture analysis: 2026-01-20
   184→
```
