     1→# External Integrations
     2→
     3→**Analysis Date:** 2026-01-20
     4→
     5→## Project Status
     6→
     7→CipherBox is a **technology demonstrator** with the following integrations implemented:
     8→
     9→- **IPFS (Kubo)**: File storage and IPNS publishing (`apps/api/src/ipfs/`)
    10→- **Web3Auth MPC Core Kit**: Authentication and key derivation (`apps/web/src/lib/web3auth/`)
    11→- **PostgreSQL**: User/vault metadata storage (`apps/api/src/`)
    12→- **Redis/BullMQ**: Job queue for background tasks (`apps/api/src/`)
    13→- **TEE (Phala Cloud)**: IPNS republishing (`tee-worker/`)
    14→
    15→## APIs & External Services
    16→
    17→**IPFS/IPNS (Implemented in PoC):**
    18→
    19→- Local IPFS daemon (Kubo) - File storage and IPNS publishing
    20→  - SDK/Client: `ipfs-http-client` 60.0.1
    21→  - Connection: `IPFS_API_URL` env var (default: <http://127.0.0.1:5001>)
    22→  - Gateway: `IPFS_GATEWAY_URL` env var (optional)
    23→  - Usage: `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/src/index.ts`
    24→
    25→**Web3Auth (Implemented):**
    26→
    27→- Authentication and key derivation - User identity
    28→  - SDK/Client: `@web3auth/mpc-core-kit` (`apps/web/src/lib/web3auth/`)
    29→  - Auth methods: Email OTP, Google OAuth, Magic Link, External Wallet
    30→  - JWKS endpoint: `https://api-auth.web3auth.io/jwks`
    31→  - Key feature: MPC-based deterministic keypair derivation with device factor MFA
    32→  - Spec: `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md` Section 2
    33→
    34→**TEE Providers (Implemented — `tee-worker/`):**
    35→
    36→- Trusted Execution Environment for IPNS republishing
    37→
    38→**Phala Cloud (Primary):**
    39→
    40→- TEE-based IPNS key decryption and signing
    41→  - Cost: ~$0.10/hr
    42→  - Features: Intel SGX hardware attestation, on-chain verification
    43→  - Latency: 12-30s per republish
    44→  - Spec: `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md` Section 9
    45→
    46→**AWS Nitro Enclaves (Fallback):**
    47→
    48→- Backup TEE provider
    49→  - Cost: ~$0.17-0.50/hr
    50→  - Features: AWS custom silicon, AWS attestation API
    51→  - Latency: <100ms per republish
    52→
    53→## Data Storage
    54→
    55→**Databases (Planned):**
    56→
    57→- PostgreSQL - User accounts, vaults, tokens, audit logs
    58→  - Tables: users, refresh_tokens, auth_nonces, vaults, volume_audit, pinned_cids, ipns_republish_schedule, tee_key_state, tee_key_rotation_log, ipfs_operations_log
    59→  - Spec: `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/API_SPECIFICATION.md` Section 4
    60→
    61→**File Storage:**
    62→
    63→- IPFS Network - Encrypted file content (decentralized)
    64→- Kubo - Local IPFS node (pinning and availability)
    65→- Local filesystem (PoC only) - State persistence in `./state/`
    66→
    67→**Caching:**
    68→
    69→- None implemented in PoC
    70→- Planned: In-memory metadata cache, disk-based encrypted content cache (desktop)
    71→
    72→## Authentication & Identity
    73→
    74→**Auth Provider (Planned):**
    75→
    76→- Web3Auth - Primary authentication
    77→  - Implementation: Two-phase auth (Web3Auth + CipherBox backend)
    78→  - Token types:
    79→    - Web3Auth ID Token (1 hour) - For backend authentication
    80→    - CipherBox Access Token (15 min) - API authorization
    81→    - CipherBox Refresh Token (7 days) - Token renewal
    82→  - Spec: `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md` Section 2
    83→
    84→**PoC Authentication:**
    85→
    86→- Local private key from `.env` - No external auth
    87→- secp256k1 keypair derived locally using `@noble/secp256k1`
    88→
    89→## Monitoring & Observability
    90→
    91→**Error Tracking:**
    92→
    93→- None implemented
    94→
    95→**Logs:**
    96→
    97→- Console logging only (PoC)
    98→- Planned: `ipfs_operations_log` table for IPFS/IPNS operation tracking
    99→
   100→**Monitoring (Planned):**
   101→
   102→- Republish success rate monitoring
   103→- TEE response latency tracking
   104→- Epoch rotation lag monitoring
   105→
   106→## CI/CD & Deployment
   107→
   108→**Hosting:**
   109→
   110→- Not deployed (PoC runs locally)
   111→- Planned: Web app hosting TBD, Backend hosting TBD
   112→
   113→**CI Pipeline:**
   114→
   115→- GitHub Actions (`.github/` directory present, contents not examined)
   116→
   117→## Environment Configuration
   118→
   119→**Required env vars (PoC):**
   120→
   121→- `ECDSA_PRIVATE_KEY` - 32-byte hex string (no 0x prefix)
   122→
   123→**Optional env vars (PoC):**
   124→
   125→- `IPFS_API_URL` - IPFS daemon endpoint
   126→- `IPFS_GATEWAY_URL` - IPFS gateway URL
   127→- `IPFS_LOCAL_API_URL` - Kubo API endpoint
   128→- `IPFS_LOCAL_GATEWAY_URL` - Kubo gateway endpoint
   129→- `POC_STATE_DIR` - State persistence directory
   130→- `IPNS_POLL_INTERVAL_MS` - Polling interval (default: 1500)
   131→- `IPNS_POLL_TIMEOUT_MS` - Polling timeout (default: 120000)
   132→- `STRESS_CHILDREN_COUNT` - Stress testing (default: 0)
   133→- `STRESS_CHILD_TYPE` - Stress test type (file/folder)
   134→
   135→**Secrets location:**
   136→
   137→- `.env` file (local development)
   138→- Environment variables (production, planned)
   139→
   140→## Webhooks & Callbacks
   141→
   142→**Incoming:**
   143→
   144→- None
   145→
   146→**Outgoing:**
   147→
   148→- None
   149→
   150→## IPFS/IPNS Integration Details
   151→
   152→**IPFS Operations (from PoC):**
   153→
   154→```typescript
   155→// Adding content
   156→const { cid } = await ctx.ipfs.add(content, { pin: false });
   157→
   158→// Fetching content
   159→const data = await collectChunks(ctx.ipfs.cat(cid));
   160→
   161→// Pinning
   162→await ctx.ipfs.pin.add(cid);
   163→await ctx.ipfs.pin.rm(cid);
   164→
   165→// Key management
   166→await ctx.ipfs.key.gen(keyName, { type: 'ed25519' });
   167→await ctx.ipfs.key.rm(keyName);
   168→```
   169→
   170→**IPNS Operations (from PoC):**
   171→
   172→```typescript
   173→// Publishing
   174→await ctx.ipfs.name.publish(`/ipfs/${cid}`, {
   175→  key: ipnsKeyName,
   176→  allowOffline: true,
   177→});
   178→
   179→// Resolving
   180→for await (const result of ctx.ipfs.name.resolve(ipnsName, { nocache: true })) {
   181→  // Extract CID from result
   182→}
   183→```
   184→
   185→**Production Relay Model (Planned):**
   186→
   187→- Client signs IPNS records locally
   188→- Backend relays signed records to IPFS network
   189→- Backend never sees plaintext IPNS private keys
   190→- Spec: `00-Preliminary-R&D/Documentation/TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md` Section 5
   191→
   192→---
   193→
   194→Integration audit: 2026-01-20
   195→
```

     1→# Technology Stack

     4→
     5→## Project Status
     6→
     7→CipherBox is a **technology demonstrator** with a working implementation. The web app (`apps/web/`), backend API (`apps/api/`), desktop app (`apps/desktop/`), shared crypto library (`packages/crypto/`), TEE worker (`tee-worker/`), and E2E test suites (`tests/e2e/`, `tests/e2e-desktop/`) are all implemented. The original PoC console harness remains in `/00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/` for reference.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.planning/codebase/ARCHITECTURE.md",
  "content": "# Architecture\n\n**Analysis Date:** 2026-03-06\n\n## Pattern Overview\n\n**Overall:** Zero-Knowledge Encrypted Cloud Storage (Implemented)\n\n**Key Characteristics:**\n\n- Layered E2E encryption with zero-knowledge server design\n- Client-side cryptographic operations with server acting as relay and storage proxy\n- Per-folder IPNS records enabling modular sharing capabilities\n- v2 FilePointer schema with per-file IPNS metadata records\n- Two-phase authentication (Web3Auth MPC Core Kit + CipherBox backend JWT)\n\n## Layers\n\n**Web Client Layer (Implemented):**\n\n- Purpose: Handle all encryption/decryption, key management, file browser UI\n- Location: `apps/web/`\n- Contains: React 18 + TypeScript + Tailwind CSS web app\n- Depends on: Web3Auth MPC Core Kit, Web Crypto API, CipherBox Backend API\n- Used by: End users via browser\n\n**Backend API Layer (Implemented):**\n\n- Purpose: Token management, IPFS/IPNS relay, vault metadata storage, TEE coordination\n- Location: `apps/api/`\n- Contains: NestJS API with auth, vault, IPFS, IPNS, shares, device-approval endpoints\n- Depends on: PostgreSQL, IPFS (Kubo), Redis/BullMQ, Web3Auth JWKS\n- Used by: Web client, desktop client\n\n**Desktop Client Layer (Implemented):**\n\n- Purpose: Transparent file access via virtual filesystem mount\n- Location: `apps/desktop/`\n- Contains: Tauri v2 shell with FUSE-T (SMB backend on macOS), WinFSP (Windows)\n- Depends on: CipherBox Backend API, FUSE-T/WinFSP\n- Used by: End users via Finder/Explorer\n\n**Shared Crypto Library (Implemented):**\n\n- Purpose: Reusable encryption/decryption primitives\n- Location: `packages/crypto/`\n- Contains: AES-256-GCM/CTR, ECIES, HKDF, metadata encryption/decryption\n- Depends on: Web Crypto API, eciesjs, @noble/ed25519\n- Used by: Web client, desktop client (via wasm/native bindings)\n\n**TEE Layer (Implemented):**\n\n- Purpose: Automatic IPNS republishing without user devices online\n- Location: `tee-worker/`\n- Contains: Phala Cloud worker for IPNS key decryption and record signing\n- Depends on: Backend republish schedule, delegated-ipfs.dev\n- Used by: Backend cron jobs (every 3 hours)\n\n**Legacy PoC (Historical Reference Only):**\n\n- Location: `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/`\n- Status: Superseded by production code. Not used in any production flow.\n\n## Data Flow\n\n**File Upload Flow:**\n\n1. Client generates random `fileKey` (256-bit AES) and IV (96-bit)\n2. Client encrypts file content with AES-256-GCM\n3. Client wraps `fileKey` with user's `publicKey` via ECIES\n4. Client sends encrypted blob to backend (POST `/ipfs/upload`, multipart/form-data field `file`)\n5. Backend relays to IPFS (Kubo), returns CID\n6. Client creates per-file FileMetadata (own IPNS record): cid, fileKeyEncrypted, fileIv, size\n7. Client adds FilePointer to folder metadata children: nameEncrypted, nameIv, fileMetaIpnsName\n8. Client encrypts folder metadata with `folderKey` (AES-256-GCM)\n9. Client signs IPNS record with folder's Ed25519 `ipnsPrivateKey`\n10. Client sends signed record to backend (POST `/ipns/publish`)\n11. Backend publishes to IPFS network via delegated routing\n\n**Authentication Flow:**\n\n1. User authenticates via Web3Auth MPC Core Kit (email OTP, Google OAuth, magic link, or external wallet)\n2. Web3Auth derives deterministic ECDSA keypair (secp256k1) via MPC threshold cryptography\n3. Client authenticates with CipherBox backend using Web3Auth ID token (JWT)\n4. Backend validates via JWKS, issues access token (15min) + refresh token (7d)\n5. Backend returns encrypted vault keys and TEE public keys\n6. Client decrypts `rootFolderKey` using `privateKey` (ECIES)\n7. Session active until logout or token expiry\n\n**State Management:**\n\n- `privateKey`: Client RAM only, never persisted or transmitted\n- `rootFolderKey`: Server stores encrypted with ECIES, client decrypts on login\n- Folder/file keys: Stored encrypted in IPNS metadata records\n- `ipnsPrivateKey`: Client encrypts with TEE public key for republishing enrollment\n\n## Key Abstractions\n\n**Vault:**\n\n- Purpose: User's encrypted file storage namespace\n- Location: `apps/api/src/vault/`, `apps/web/src/stores/vault.store.ts`\n- Pattern: One vault per user, identified by `rootIpnsName`\n\n**Folder:**\n\n- Purpose: Encrypted container with child entries (files/folders)\n- Location: `apps/web/src/services/folder.service.ts`, `packages/crypto/src/metadata/`\n- Pattern: Each folder has own IPNS keypair, `folderKey` for metadata encryption\n\n**FilePointer (v2):**\n\n- Purpose: Slim reference in folder metadata pointing to per-file IPNS record\n- Location: `packages/crypto/src/metadata/types.ts`\n- Pattern: Contains encrypted name, timestamps, `fileMetaIpnsName` (reference to FileMetadata)\n\n**FileMetadata:**\n\n- Purpose: Per-file crypto material in dedicated IPNS record\n- Location: `packages/crypto/src/metadata/types.ts`\n- Pattern: Contains CID, `fileKeyEncrypted`, `fileIv`, size, encryption mode\n\n**TEE Key Epoch:**\n\n- Purpose: Rotation-safe TEE public key management\n- Location: `apps/api/src/republish/`, `tee-worker/src/`\n- Pattern: Client encrypts IPNS keys with current epoch, TEE supports previous for 4-week grace period\n\n## Entry Points\n\n**Web Application:**\n\n- Location: `apps/web/src/main.tsx`\n- Dev: `pnpm --filter web dev` (http://localhost:5173)\n- Routes: `/auth`, `/vault`, file browser UI\n\n**Backend API:**\n\n- Location: `apps/api/src/main.ts`\n- Dev: `pnpm --filter api dev` (http://localhost:3000)\n- Endpoints: auth, vault, IPFS, IPNS, shares, device-approval, republish\n\n**Desktop Application:**\n\n- Location: `apps/desktop/src/main.ts` (Tauri frontend), `apps/desktop/src-tauri/` (Rust backend)\n- Dev: `pnpm --filter desktop tauri dev`\n- Mount point: `~/CipherBox` (macOS FUSE-T SMB)\n\n**TEE Worker:**\n\n- Location: `tee-worker/src/index.ts`\n- Deployed to Phala Cloud\n\n## Error Handling\n\n**Strategy:** Fail-fast with user-facing error messages\n\n**Patterns:**\n\n- Web: Toast notifications for user errors, console.error for debug (see CONCERNS.md for logging tech debt)\n- API: HTTP status codes with structured NestJS exception filters\n- Desktop FUSE: Returns errno codes (EIO, ENOENT, EPERM) to kernel\n- IPNS delegated routing: Exponential backoff with retry (3 attempts, 1s base, 30s cap)\n- Token refresh: Automatic retry with queue deduplication (`apps/web/src/lib/api/client.ts`)\n\n## Cross-Cutting Concerns\n\n**Logging:**\n\n- API: NestJS Logger (structured)\n- Web: Direct console.* calls (tech debt — see CONCERNS.md)\n- Desktop: Rust `log` crate with `env_logger`\n\n**Authentication:**\n\n- Two-phase: Web3Auth MPC Core Kit for key derivation, CipherBox backend for API tokens\n- Token rotation: Refresh tokens rotated on each use\n- Zero-knowledge: Backend never sees private keys or plaintext\n\n**Security Invariants:**\n\n- Private key exists only in client RAM, cleared on logout\n- All files encrypted with unique random key + IV (no deduplication)\n- Server stores only encrypted keys (ECIES wrapped)\n- TEE keys exist in enclave memory only during signing, then zeroed\n\n---\n\nArchitecture analysis: 2026-03-06\n"
}

  "content": "# External Integrations\n\n**Analysis Date:** 2026-03-06\n\n## Project Status\n\nCipherBox is a **technology demonstrator** with the following integrations implemented:\n\n- **IPFS (Kubo)**: File storage and IPNS publishing (`apps/api/src/ipfs/`)\n- **Web3Auth MPC Core Kit**: Authentication and key derivation (`apps/web/src/lib/web3auth/`)\n- **PostgreSQL**: User/vault metadata storage (`apps/api/src/`)\n- **Redis/BullMQ**: Job queue for background tasks (`apps/api/src/`)\n- **TEE (Phala Cloud)**: IPNS republishing (`tee-worker/`)\n\n## APIs & External Services\n\n**IPFS/IPNS (Implemented):**\n\n- Local IPFS daemon (Kubo) - File storage and IPNS publishing\n  - Provider: `apps/api/src/ipfs/providers/local.provider.ts`\n  - Connection: `KUBO_API_URL` env var (default: `http://127.0.0.1:5001`)\n  - Upload: `POST /ipfs/upload` (multipart/form-data, field `file`)\n  - Fetch: `GET /ipfs/:cid`\n  - Unpin: `POST /ipfs/unpin`\n\n- Delegated routing (delegated-ipfs.dev) - IPNS record publishing and resolution\n  - Client: `apps/api/src/ipns/delegated-routing.client.ts`\n  - Publish: `POST /ipns/publish`, `POST /ipns/publish-batch`\n  - Resolve: `GET /ipns/resolve`\n  - Retry: Exponential backoff (3 retries, 1s base, 30s cap)\n\n**Web3Auth (Implemented):**\n\n- Authentication and key derivation - User identity\n  - SDK/Client: `@web3auth/mpc-core-kit` (`apps/web/src/lib/web3auth/`)\n  - Auth methods: Email OTP, Google OAuth, Magic Link, External Wallet\n  - JWKS endpoint: `https://api-auth.web3auth.io/jwks`\n  - Backend validation: `apps/api/src/auth/strategies/web3auth-jwt.strategy.ts`\n  - Key feature: MPC-based deterministic keypair derivation with device factor MFA\n\n**TEE Providers (Implemented):**\n\n- Trusted Execution Environment for IPNS republishing\n\n**Phala Cloud (Primary — Implemented):**\n\n- TEE-based IPNS key decryption and signing\n  - Worker: `tee-worker/src/`\n  - Features: Intel SGX hardware attestation\n  - Schedule: Every 3 hours via backend cron\n  - Enrollment: `apps/api/src/republish/republish.service.ts`\n\n**AWS Nitro Enclaves (Planned Fallback):**\n\n- Backup TEE provider (not yet implemented)\n\n## Data Storage\n\n**PostgreSQL (Implemented):**\n\n- User accounts, vaults, tokens, audit logs\n  - ORM: TypeORM (`apps/api/src/`)\n  - Migrations: `apps/api/src/migrations/`\n  - Key entities: users, vaults, refresh_tokens, pinned_cids, ipns_republish_schedule, shares, device_approvals\n  - Protocol: `docs/DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md`\n\n**Redis (Implemented):**\n\n- BullMQ job queue for background tasks\n  - Connection: `REDIS_URL` env var\n  - Used by: `apps/api/src/` for async job processing\n\n**IPFS (Implemented):**\n\n- Encrypted file content storage (decentralized)\n  - Kubo node for pinning and availability\n  - All content is ciphertext (zero-knowledge)\n\n**Caching:**\n\n- API: In-memory IPNS resolution cache with DB-cached CID fallback\n- Desktop: In-memory metadata cache with background refresh (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/`)\n\n## Authentication & Identity\n\n**Web3Auth (Implemented):**\n\n- Primary authentication and key derivation\n  - Implementation: Two-phase auth (Web3Auth MPC Core Kit + CipherBox backend JWT)\n  - Token types:\n    - Web3Auth ID Token (1 hour) - For backend authentication\n    - CipherBox Access Token (15 min) - API authorization\n    - CipherBox Refresh Token (7 days) - Token renewal\n  - Details: `docs/AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md`\n\n**Test Authentication (Dev/Staging Only):**\n\n- `POST /auth/test-login` - Bypasses real auth for E2E testing\n  - Guarded by `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` env var and `NODE_ENV !== 'production'`\n\n## Monitoring & Observability\n\n**API Metrics (Implemented):**\n\n- Prometheus metrics: `apps/api/src/metrics/`\n- Health check: `GET /health`\n\n**Web App:**\n\n- No error tracking service (see CONCERNS.md)\n- Console logging only\n\n**Logs:**\n\n- API: NestJS structured logger\n- Web: Console.* calls (tech debt)\n- Desktop: Rust `log` crate\n\n## CI/CD & Deployment\n\n**CI Pipeline (Implemented):**\n\n- GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/`)\n  -   "content": "# Technology Stack\n\n**Analysis Date:** 2026-03-06\n\n## Project Status\n\nCipherBox is a **technology demonstrator** with a working implementation. The original PoC console harness remains in `00-Preliminary-R&D/poc/` for historical reference only.\n\n## Languages\n\n**Primary:**\n\n- TypeScript 5.7+ - All application code (web, API, desktop frontend, crypto, TEE worker)\n\n**Secondary:**\n\n- Rust - Desktop FUSE filesystem (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/`)\n- SQL - PostgreSQL migrations (`apps/api/src/migrations/`)\n- JavaScript (ES2022) - Compilation target\n\n## Runtime\n\n**Environment:**\n\n- Node.js 20+ - Backend API and build tooling\n- Browser (Chrome/Firefox/Safari) - Web app\n- Tauri v2 (WebView + Rust) - Desktop app (macOS, Windows)\n\n**Package Manager:**\n\n- pnpm 9+ - Workspace-based monorepo management\n- Lockfile: `pnpm-lock.yaml` at workspace root\n\n## Frameworks\n\n**Web App (`apps/web/`):**\n\n- React 18 - Frontend framework\n- Tailwind CSS - Styling\n- Vite - Build tooling and dev server\n- Zustand - State management\n\n**Backend (`apps/api/`):**\n\n- NestJS - Backend framework\n- TypeORM - Database ORM with migrations\n- BullMQ + Redis - Job queue for background tasks\n- Passport - Authentication strategies\n\n**Desktop (`apps/desktop/`):**\n\n- Tauri v2 - Desktop shell (Rust + WebView)\n- FUSE-T (SMB backend) - Virtual filesystem mount (macOS)\n- WinFSP - Virtual filesystem (Windows)\n- Vendored fuser crate - FUSE bindings with socket-read patch\n\n**Build/Dev:**\n\n- TypeScript 5.7+ - Type checking and compilation\n- ESLint 9 - Linting (flat config)\n- Vitest - Unit testing (web, crypto)\n- Jest - Unit testing (API)\n- Playwright - E2E testing\n\n## Key Dependencies\n\n**Cryptography:**\n\n- `eciesjs` ^0.4.16 - ECIES encryption (secp256k1 key wrapping)\n- `@noble/secp256k1` - Public key derivation\n- `@noble/ed25519` - Ed25519 IPNS record signing\n- Web Crypto API - AES-256-GCM/CTR encryption, HKDF-SHA256 key derivation\n\n**Authentication:**\n\n- `@web3auth/mpc-core-kit` ^3.5.0 - MPC-based auth and deterministic keypair derivation\n- `jose` - JWT verification (backend JWKS validation)\n- `@simplewebauthn/*` - WebAuthn/passkey support\n\n**IPFS/IPNS:**\n\n- Kubo HTTP API - File storage and pinning (via `apps/api/src/ipfs/providers/local.provider.ts`)\n- delegated-ipfs.dev - IPNS record publishing and resolution\n\n**Desktop (Rust):**\n\n- `fuser` (vendored) - FUSE bindings with FUSE-T socket-read patch\n- `winfsp` - Windows filesystem in userspace\n- `reqwest` - HTTP client for API calls\n- `keyring` - OS keychain for token storage\n- `tauri` v2 - Desktop app framework\n\n## Configuration\n\n**Environment:**\n\n- `.env` files for local configuration (see `apps/api/.env.example`, `apps/web/.env.example`)\n- GitHub Actions secrets/vars for CI/CD\n- Docker Compose `.env` for staging\n\n**TypeScript:**\n\n- Target: ES2022\n- Module: ES2022 / ESNext (varies by workspace)\n- ModuleResolution: Bundler\n- Strict mode enabled across all workspaces\n\n**Build Commands:**\n\n- `pnpm dev` - Start all dev servers\n- `pnpm --filter api dev` - API dev server (http://localhost:3000)\n- `pnpm --filter web dev` - Web dev server (http://localhost:5173)\n- `pnpm --filter desktop tauri dev` - Desktop dev\n- `pnpm test` - Run unit tests (all workspaces)\n- `pnpm typecheck` - TypeScript type checking\n- `pnpm api:generate` - Regenerate API client from OpenAPI spec\n\n## Cryptography Stack\n\n**Symmetric Encryption:**\n\n- AES-256-GCM - File and metadata encryption (Web Crypto API)\n- AES-256-CTR - Streaming encryption for large files and media playback (Web Crypto API)\n\n**Asymmetric Encryption:**\n\n- ECIES (secp256k1) - Key wrapping via `eciesjs`\n- ECDSA (secp256k1) - Keypair from Web3Auth MPC Core Kit\n- Ed25519 - IPNS record signing via `@noble/ed25519`\n\n**Key Derivation:**\n\n- HKDF-SHA256 - Deterministic IPNS keypair derivation from user's private key (Web Crypto API)\n- Random generation - Content encryption keys (file keys, folder keys) via `crypto.getRandomValues()`\n\n## Platform Requirements\n\n**Development:**\n\n- Node.js 20+\n- pnpm 9+\n- PostgreSQL 16 (Docker or local)\n- Redis (Docker or local)\n- IPFS Kubo node with HTTP API enabled\n\n**Staging/Production:**\n\n- Docker Compose for orchestration\n- PostgreSQL database\n- Redis instance\n- IPFS Kubo node\n- Web3Auth project (auth)\n- Phala Cloud TEE worker (IPNS republishing)\n\n---\n\nStack analysis: 2026-03-06\n"

  "description": "Get database IDs for unresolved comments"
}

    28→
    29→CipherBox uses a **mandatory two-phase auth flow**:
    30→
    31→1. **Phase 1 (Web3Auth MPC Core Kit):** User authenticates via email OTP, Google OAuth, magic link, or external wallet. Web3Auth derives an ECDSA secp256k1 keypair via MPC threshold cryptography. MFA is handled via device factors.
    32→2. **Phase 2 (CipherBox Backend):** Client authenticates with the backend using a Web3Auth ID token (JWT) validated via JWKS. The backend issues its own access/refresh token pair.
    33→
    34→**Key Insight:** The ECDSA keypair from Phase 1 is the user's identity. Web3Auth ensures the same user always derives the same keypair regardless of auth method (when methods resolve to the same CipherBox `userId`).
    35→
    36→See [docs/AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md](../docs/AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md) for full details.
    37→
    38→### Zero-Knowledge Key Hierarchy
    39→
    40→```text
    41→User Auth → Web3Auth MPC Core Kit → ECDSA Private Key (client RAM only, never transmitted)
    42→                    ├─ Used for ECIES decryption of all data keys
```

     1→# Authentication Architecture
     2→
     3→This document describes the Web3Auth MPC Core Kit key architecture as integrated into CipherBox, covering the default account state, factor lifecycle, and security implications.
     4→
     5→## Table of Contents
     6→
     7→1. [Overview](#1-overview)
     8→2. [Authentication Flow](#2-authentication-flow)
     9→3. [Default Account State (Pre-MFA)](#3-default-account-state-pre-mfa)
    10→4. [Factor Lifecycle After enableMFA()](#4-factor-lifecycle-after-enablemfa)
    11→5. [Security Analysis](#5-security-analysis)
    12→6. [Cross-Device Approval](#6-cross-device-approval)
    13→7. [MFA Status Detection](#7-mfa-status-detection)
    14→8. [Key Files Reference](#8-key-files-reference)
    15→
    16→---
    17→
    18→## 1. Overview
    19→
    20→CipherBox uses Web3Auth's MPC Core Kit to derive a deterministic secp256k1 ECDSA keypair from user authentication. This keypair is the root of CipherBox's encryption hierarchy -- the private key decrypts the vault's root folder key, IPNS signing keys, and all downstream file/folder keys.
    21→
    22→The MPC Core Kit uses **Threshold Secret Sharing (TSS)** to split the private key into multiple **factors** distributed across different parties. While TSS supports distributed signing without reconstructing the key, CipherBox needs the full private key client-side for ECIES decryption and IPNS signing, so it calls `_UNSAFE_exportTssKey()` each session to reconstruct the key locally from the available factors. The exported key exists only in browser memory and is destroyed on logout.
    23→
    24→```text
    25→User Authentication
    26→    │
    27→    ▼
    28→Web3Auth MPC Core Kit (TSS)
    29→    │
    30→    ├── Factor 1: JWT verifier share (distributed across Web3Auth DKG nodes)
    31→    ├── Factor 2: hashedShare (Web3Auth metadata server)
    32→    │   [After enableMFA(): deleted and replaced with:]
    33→    ├── Factor 3: Device share (browser localStorage)
    34→    └── Factor 4: Recovery share (user-held 24-word mnemonic)
    35→    │
    36→    ▼
    37→secp256k1 ECDSA Keypair
    38→    │
    39→    ├── privateKey → decrypts rootFolderKey, ipnsPrivateKey, folderKeys, fileKeys
    40→    └── publicKey  → identifies user, encrypts all data keys via ECIES
    41→```
    42→
    43→---
    44→
    45→## 2. Authentication Flow
    46→
    47→CipherBox does **not** use Web3Auth's built-in social login directly. Instead, it implements a custom verifier flow:
    48→
    49→### 2.1 Custom Verifier: `cipherbox-identity`
    50→

    47→CipherBox does **not** use Web3Auth's built-in social login directly. Instead, it implements a custom verifier flow:
    48→
    49→### 2.1 Custom Verifier: `cipherbox-identity`
    50→
    51→The CipherBox backend authenticates users (via Google OAuth, email OTP, or wallet SIWE signature) and issues a short-lived RS256 JWT:
    52→
    53→```text
    54→Claims: { sub: userId, iss: "cipherbox", aud: "web3auth", exp: "5m" }
    55→Signing: RS256 with kid "cipherbox-identity-1"
    56→JWKS endpoint: /.well-known/jwks.json (served by CipherBox backend)
    57→```
    58→
    59→This JWT is passed to Core Kit's `loginWithJWT()`:
    60→
    61→```typescript
    62→await coreKit.loginWithJWT({
    63→  verifier: 'cipherbox-identity', // Custom verifier registered with Web3Auth
    64→  verifierId: userId, // Unique user identifier
    65→  idToken: cipherboxJwt, // JWT from CipherBox backend
    66→});
    67→```
    68→
    69→Web3Auth's network validates the JWT against the JWKS endpoint and uses the verified `verifierId` to deterministically derive the user's TSS key shares.
    70→
    71→### 2.2 Key Derivation
    72→
    73→The verifier identity (`cipherbox-identity` + `userId`) deterministically maps to a secp256k1 keypair via Web3Auth's Distributed Key Generation (DKG) protocol. The same user always derives the same keypair regardless of which authentication method (Google, email, wallet) they use, as long as the CipherBox backend resolves them to the same `userId`.
    74→
    75→---
    76→
    77→## 3. Default Account State (Pre-MFA)
    78→
    79→When a user signs up and authenticates for the first time, Core Kit's DKG protocol creates two factors:
    80→
    81→### 3.1 Factor Breakdown
    82→
    83→| #   | Factor             | Module                                 | Storage                               | Controlled By    |
    84→| --- | ------------------ | -------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- | ---------------- |
    85→| 1   | JWT verifier share | (internal, not in `shareDescriptions`) | Distributed across Web3Auth DKG nodes | Web3Auth network |
    86→| 2   | hashedShare        | `hashedShare`                          | Web3Auth metadata server              | Web3Auth         |
```

> AGENT

Triage:

1. **Thread 1 (auth identity convergence, line 34):** The auth doc at line 73 explicitly says "The same user always derives the same keypair regardless of which authentication method they use, **as long as the CipherBox backend resolves them to the same `userId`**." This is the correct behavior — the backend controls identity resolution. CodeRabbit's claim that "methods are independent identities until the user explicitly links them" is CodeRabbit's assumption, not how the system works. The current wording already qualifies with "when methods resolve to the same CipherBox `userId`". However, I can clarify slightly to make it less ambiguous.

2. **Thread 2 (AES-256-CTR in primitives, line 85):** CTR is implemented (`packages/crypto/src/aes/encrypt-ctr.ts`, `decrypt-ctr.ts`) and actively used for streaming media playback and desktop FUSE reads. It belongs in the primitives table. CodeRabbit's suggestion to remove it is incorrect.

Let me make a minor clarification for thread 1 and push back on thread 2.

[docs/DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md](../docs/DATABASE_EVOLUTION_PROTOCOL.md) | Migration discipline                                           |\n \n ---\n \n ## Critical Architecture Patterns\n \n ### Two-Phase Authentication Model\n \n-CipherBox uses a **mandatory two-phase auth flow** that must be understood before implementing any auth-related features:\n+CipherBox uses a **mandatory two-phase auth flow**:\n+\n+1. **Phase 1 (Web3Auth MPC Core Kit):** User authenticates via email OTP, Google OAuth, magic link, or external wallet. Web3Auth derives an ECDSA secp256k1 keypair via MPC threshold cryptography. MFA is handled via device factors.\n+2. **Phase 2 (CipherBox Backend):** Client authenticates with the backend using a Web3Auth ID token (JWT) validated via JWKS. The backend issues its own access/refresh token pair.\n \n-1. **Phase 1 (Web3Auth):** User authenticates → Web3Auth derives ECDSA secp256k1 keypair via threshold cryptography\n-2. **Phase 2 (CipherBox Backend):** Client authenticates with backend using either:\n-   - **Option A:** Web3Auth ID token (JWT) validated via JWKS endpoint\n-   - **Option B:** SIWE-like signature flow (nonce-based)\n+**Key Insight:** The ECDSA keypair from Phase 1 is the user's identity. Web3Auth ensures the same user always derives the same keypair regardless of auth method (when methods resolve to the same CipherBox `userId`).\n \n-**Key Insight:** The ECDSA keypair from Phase 1 is the user's identity. Web3Auth's \"group connections\" feature ensures that Google OAuth, email/password, magic links, and external wallet auth all derive the **same keypair** for the same user.\n+See [docs/AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md](../docs/AUTHENTICATION_ARCHITECTURE.md) for full details.\n \n ### Zero-Knowledge Key Hierarchy\n \n-```\n-User Auth → Web3Auth → ECDSA Private Key (client RAM only, never transmitted)\n+```text\n+User Auth → Web3Auth MPC Core Kit → ECDSA Private Key (client RAM only, never transmitted)\n                     ├─ Used for ECIES decryption of all data keys\n-                    ├─ Used to decrypt IPNS signing keys (Ed25519)\n+                    ├─ Used to derive IPNS signing keys (Ed25519, HKDF)\n                     └─ Destroyed on logout\n \n ECDSA Public Key (stored on server, identifies user)\n     └─ Used to encrypt (ECIES):\n-        ├─ Root Folder Key (AES-256, stored encrypted on server)\n-        ├─ Subfolder Keys (AES-256, stored encrypted in parent IPNS metadata)\n-        └─ File Keys (AES-256, stored encrypted in folder IPNS metadata)\n+        ├─ Root Folder Key (random AES-256, stored encrypted on server)\n+        ├─ Subfolder Keys (random AES-256, stored encrypted in parent folder metadata)\n+        └─ File Keys (random AES-256, stored encrypted in per-file metadata)\n+\n+IPNS Keypairs (Ed25519, HKDF-derived from private key)\n+    ├─ Vault IPNS (root folder metadata pointer)\n+    ├─ Device Registry IPNS\n+    └─ Per-file IPNS (file metadata pointer)\n ```\n \n **Critical Rule:** Never log, persist to disk, or transmit the ECDSA private key. It exists ONLY in client memory during the session.\n \n-### Per-Folder IPNS Architecture\n-\n-Each folder has its own IPNS entry containing encrypted metadata. This design enables future per-folder sharing (v2+).\n+### v2 Folder Metadata with FilePointers\n \n-**Folder Metadata Structure:**\n+Each folder has its own IPNS entry. Files use a v2 `FilePointer` schema — the folder stores a slim reference, and per-file crypto material lives in a dedicated IPNS record.\n \n-```typescript\n-interface DecryptedFolderMetadata {\n-  children: Array<{\n-    type: 'file' | 'folder';\n-    nameEncrypted: string; // AES-256-GCM(name, folderKey)\n-    nameIv: string;\n-    cid?: string; // For files: IPFS CID\n-    ipnsName?: string; // For folders: IPNS name\n-    fileKeyEncrypted?: string; // ECIES(fileKey, userPubkey)\n-    subfolderKeyEncrypted?: string; // ECIES(folderKey, userPubkey)\n-    size?: number;\n-    created: number;\n-    modified: number;\n-  }>;\n-}\n-```\n+See [docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md](../docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md) for the full schema reference including `FolderMetadata`, `FilePointer`, `FileMetadata`, and all other metadata objects.\n \n-**When modifying files/folders:** Always re-encrypt and republish the containing folder's IPNS entry.\n+## Technology Stack\n \n-## Technology Stack & Standards\n+| Component          | Technology                                       |\n+| :----------------- | :----------------------------------------------- |\n+| **Frontend**       | React 18 + TypeScript + Tailwind CSS             |\n+| **Backend**        | Node.js + NestJS + TypeScript                    |\n+| **Database**       | PostgreSQL 16                                    |\n+| **Job Queue**      | BullMQ + Redis                                   |\n+| **Key Derivation** | Web3Auth MPC Core Kit (`@web3auth/mpc-core-kit`) |\n+| **Storage**        | IPFS via Kubo                                    |\n+| **Desktop**        | Tauri v2 + FUSE-T (macOS, SMB backend)           |\n+| **TEE**            | Phala Cloud (IPNS republishing)                  |\n+| **Crypto**         | Web Crypto API (AES-256-GCM, ECIES secp256k1)    |\n \n ### Encryption Primitives (Non-Negotiable)\n \n-| Algorithm           | Use Case                            | Implementation                |\n-| ------------------- | ----------------------------------- | ----------------------------- |\n-| **AES-256-GCM**     | File content + metadata encryption  | Web Crypto API / libsodium.js |\n-| **ECIES secp256k1** | Key wrapping (files, folders)       | ethers.js / libsodium.js      |\n-| **ECDSA secp256k1** | IPNS signing, user identity         | Web3Auth / ethers.js          |\n-| **HKDF-SHA256**     | Key derivation                      | Web Crypto API                |\n-| **Argon2**          | Password hashing (server-side only) | argon2-browser                |\n+| Algorithm           | Use Case                            | Implementation |\n+| ------------------- | ----------------------------------- | -------------- |\n+| **AES-256-GCM**     | File content + metadata encryption  | Web Crypto API |\n+| **AES-256-CTR**     | Streaming encryption (large files)  | Web Crypto API |\n+| **ECIES secp256k1** | Key wrapping (files, folders, IPNS) | eciesjs        |\n+| **HKDF-SHA256**     | IPNS keypair derivation             | Web Crypto API |\n+| **Ed25519**         | IPNS record signing                 | @noble/ed25519 |\n 11:- Impact: Orphaned IPNS records accumulate in the TEE republish schedule. The republish service has capacity warnings at 1000+ records. Each orphan wastes TEE compute and delegated routing bandwidth every 3 hours until natural expiry.
170:- Current capacity: TEE republishes all enrolled IPNS records every 3 hours via batch endpoint.
